BMS18
, Lemma 6.10]
. For each
m\geq 0
, Lemma 6.9 in loc.cit. provides a map
(\mu)^{\otimes m}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}\tau^{[0,m]}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}\to\tau^{[0,m]}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
which is part of the compositions

(\mu)^{\otimes m}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}\tau^{[0,m]}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to(\mu)^{\otimes m}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}\tau^{[0,m]}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}\to\tau^{[0,m]}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

(3.6)

and

(\mu)^{\otimes m}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}\tau^{[0,m]}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}\to\tau^{[0,m]}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to\tau^{[0,m]}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}

(3.7)

Moreover both (
3.6
)-(
3.7
) are just the identity tensored with the inclusion
(\mu)^{\otimes m}\hookrightarrow A_{\Inf}
. Let
C_{m}
be the cone of the truncated map
\tau^{[0,m]}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to\tau^{[0,m]}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
. Then by (
3.6
)-(
3.7
),
H^{i}(C_{m})
is killed by
\mu^{2m}
and so
H^{m}(C^{\bullet})
is killed by
\mu^{2m+2}
. The Tor-spectral sequence then shows that
H^{m}(C^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
is killed by
\mu^{4m+6}
for each
n\geq 1
. By functoriality one obtains
R^{i}\varprojlim_{n}(H^{m}(C^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))
is also killed by
\mu^{4m+6}
for all
i\geq 0,n\geq 1
. The spectral sequence

R^{i}\varprojlim_{n}(H^{m}(C^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))\implies H^{i+m}(R\varprojlim_{n}(C^{\bullet}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))

then shows that
H^{m}(C^{\bullet\wedge})
is killed by some power of
\mu
, where
C^{\bullet\wedge}
is the derived
p
-adic completion of
C^{\bullet}
. Finally note that the cone of (
3.5
) is
C^{\bullet\wedge}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]
which is thus quasi-isomorphic to the zero complex.
∎

For integral
p
-adic étale cohomology, we will use the framework established in
[
Sch13
, §8]
. Recall the morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
gives rise to a morphism of pro-étale topoi on the generic fiber:

f_{\eta,\proet}\colon X_{\proet}\to Y_{\proet}.

This gives rise to
relative
integral
p
-adic étale cohomology (cf. Proposition 8.2 in loc.cit.)

Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}.

(3.8)

The following lemma says that the complex
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is well behaved.

Lemma 3.12
.

The complex
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\in D(Y_{\proet},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is adic lisse (cf. Definition
3.5
).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.8
, it suffices to show
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\in D(Y_{\proet},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is lisse. We need to check that the conditions of Definition
3.1
are satisifed. By
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
it is
p
-adically derived complete. Moreover by the projection formula (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0944]
) and
[
Sch13
, Corollary 3.17(ii)]

Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}/p\cong\nu^{*}Rf_{\eta,\et*}(\mathbb{Z}/p),

where
\nu\colon Y_{\proet}\to Y_{\et}
is the natural morphism of sites. By
[
SW20
, Theorem 10.5.1]
,
Rf_{\eta,\et*}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
has finite locally constant cohomology sheaves. Furthermore by base change (cf.
[
Hub96
, Proposition 2.6.1]
) and vanishing of cohomology in large enough degrees (cf.
[
Sch13
, Theorem 5.1]
),
Rf_{\eta,\et*}(\mathbb{Z}/p)
has finite tor-dimension. This proves the lemma.
∎

We need an analogue of
[
BMS18
, Theorem 5.7]
. First we need some preparation. For a complex
\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules (in a ringed site), denote by
\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n}:=\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

Suppose
\mathscr{A}^{\bullet}\to\mathscr{B}^{\bullet}
is a morphism in
D(Y_{\proet},A_{\Inf})
of
p
-adically derived complete
A_{\Inf}
-complexes such that the induced morphism

H^{i}(\mathscr{A}^{\bullet}_{n})(U)\to H^{i}(\mathscr{B}^{\bullet}_{n})(U)

is an almost isomorphism (w.r.t.
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
) for all
i
,
n\geq 1
and
U\in Y_{\proet}
. Then the cohomology sheaves of the cone of
\mathscr{A}^{\bullet}\to\mathscr{B}^{\bullet}
are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be the cone. We will prove the statement in four steps.

(1)

“
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
kills
H^{i}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})(U)
”. Note that
H^{i}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})
sits in an exact sequence

H^{i}(\mathscr{A}^{\bullet}_{n})\to H^{i}(\mathscr{B}^{\bullet}_{n})\to H^{i}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})\to H^{i+1}(\mathscr{A}^{\bullet}_{n})\to H^{i+1}(\mathscr{B}^{\bullet}_{n}).

(3.9)

The kernel and cokernel presheaves of the first and last arrows of (
3.9
) are killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
. Since sheafification preserves the property

``\text{local sections killed by }[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]"

(3.10)

it follows that the cokernel of the first and last arrows is killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
. It is easy to check that (
3.10
) is also stable under extensions and this proves that
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
kills
H^{i}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})(U)
.

(2)

“
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
kills
H^{i}(U,\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})
”. By the Leray spectral sequence (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0732]
)

H^{p}(U,H^{q}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n}))\implies H^{p+q}(U,\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n}),

it suffices to show that each
H^{p}(U,H^{q}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n}))
is killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
.
Step
1 says that this is true for
p=0
. In other words for
m\in[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
, the multiplcation map

m\colon H^{q}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})\to H^{q}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n})

is zero. By functorality, this implies that the induced map

m\colon H^{p}(U,H^{q}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n}))\to H^{p}(U,H^{q}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{n}))

is also zero.

(3)

“
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
kills
H^{i}(U,\mathscr{C}^{\bullet})
”. By
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
and
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.15]
,
H^{i}(U,\mathscr{C}^{\bullet})
is
p
-adically derived complete. Thus by
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.17]
, it suffices to check that it is killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
. This follows from
Step
2 and
[
Sta18
, Tag 0D6K]
(note that the
R^{1}\varprojlim
of almost zero modules is almost zero).

(4)

“
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
kills
H^{i}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet})(U)
”. This follows from
Step
3 and the fact that
H^{i}(\mathscr{C}^{\bullet})
is the sheafification of
U\mapsto H^{i}(U,\mathscr{C}^{\bullet})
.

∎

Proposition 3.14
.

The morphism
f_{\eta,\proet}^{-1}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,Y}\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
induces a morphism

Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,Y}\to Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}

(3.11)

such that the cohomology sheaves of its cone are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
.

Proof.

We check that the conditions of Lemma
3.13
are satisifed. By Lemma
3.12
,
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is an adic lisse complex on
Y_{\proet}
. It follows that the LHS of (
3.11
) is
p
-adically derived complete. By
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
the RHS of (
3.11
),
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
is
p
-adically derived complete.

Finally after tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
and taking cohomology, (
3.11
) takes the shape

\nu^{*}R^{i}f_{\eta,\et*}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})\to R^{i}f_{\eta,\proet*}(W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X})/p^{n}).

(3.12)

The proof of
[
Sch13
, Theorem 8.8]
shows that (after taking sections) (
3.12
) is an almost isomorphism (w.r.t to
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
).
∎

After inverting
\mu
, the relative
A_{\Inf}
-cohomology
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
(cf. Definition
2.17
) and its derived
p
-adic completion capture (
3.8
). More precisely:

Theorem 3.15
.

We have the identifications

R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]\cong R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})^{\wedge}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]\cong Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,Y}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}].

Proof.

First we show the left-most quasi-isomorphism. Indeed Lemmas
3.10
and
3.11
imply that the canonical morphism

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]\to A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\wedge}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]

is a quasi-isomorphism. The result now follows from
[
SGA4
, Exposé VI, Théorème 5.1]
(applied to Proposition
2.7
(4)) and
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
.

It remains to compare the two outer terms.
Since
\mu\in W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
, this is a consequence of Lemma
3.10
, Proposition
3.14
and
[
SGA4
, Exposé VI, Théorème 5.1]
(again applied to Proposition
2.7
(4)).
∎

4
The local analysis of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

Let
R
and
S
be flat
\mathcal{O}
-algebras equipped with the
p
-adic topology such that
R[p^{-1}]
and
S[p^{-1}]
are strongly noetherian. Moreover we assume
R
and
S
are complete. We assume throughout §
4
that
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(S)
,
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
and
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is any smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism such that for some
d\geq 0
, there is an étale
\Spf(S)
-morphism

\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)\rightarrow\Spf(R^{\square})=:\mathfrak{X}^{\square}

where
R^{\square}:=S\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{d}^{\pm 1}\}
. Let
f_{\eta}\colon X\to Y
denote the induced morphism on the adic generic fibers. Armed with Definition
2.17
, the first goal of this section is to relate the cohomology of the complex
Rf_{\eta*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
, in terms of continuous group cohomology (cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 1.16]
in the smooth case and
[
CK19
, Theorem 3.20]
in the semistable case), particularly after killing
\mu
-torsion. The origin of such a computation goes back to ideas of Faltings. The main steps are analogous to those appearing in the absolute case (cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 1.16]
in the smooth case and
[
CK19
, Theorem 3.20]
in the semistable case). The main difference in the current situation is that the pro-étale site of the target
Y
adds some extra technicalities.

4.1
The perfectoid cover
X_{\infty}

We use the setup in
[
BMS18
, §5.1]
when dealing with the pro-étale site associated to a locally noetherian adic space. We fix some pro-étale morphism

Y_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\rightarrow Y,

where
Y_{\infty}
is some affinoid perfectoid object in
Y_{\proet}
. Setting
S_{\infty}:=(\varinjlim_{i}S_{i}^{+})^{\wedge}
where the completion is
p
-adic one has the following fact which is implicit in loc.cit.:

Lemma 4.1
.

S_{\infty}
is a torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-module.

Proof.

Note first that each
S_{i}
is a
C
-vector space and
S_{i}^{+}\subset S_{i}
is a subring and therefore
S_{i}^{+}
is a torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-module. The direct limit of torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-modules is again torsion free and so
\varinjlim_{i}S_{i}^{+}
is a torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-module. Since
\mathcal{O}
is a valuation ring by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0539]
, this is equivalent to
\varinjlim_{i}S_{i}^{+}
being a flat
\mathcal{O}
-module. One then applies
[
GR03
, Lemma 7.1.6(i)]
to conclude that the
p
-adic completion
S_{\infty}
is a flat and therefore a torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-module.
∎

The pro-object
Y_{\infty}
therefore determines the perfectoid space
\Spa(S_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}],S_{\infty})
. Base changing along
Y_{\infty}
, we will construct a perfectoid cover over
X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}
. More precisely, for each
m\geq 0
, we consider the
R^{\square}
-algebra

R^{\square}_{m}:=S_{\infty}\{t_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{m}},\ldots,t_{d}^{\pm 1/p^{m}}\}\text{ and }R_{\infty}^{\square}:=(\varinjlim R^{\square}_{m})^{\wedge},

(4.1)

where the completion is
p
-adic. Explicitly, we have the
p
-adically completed direct sum decomposition

R_{\infty}^{\square}\cong\widehat{\bigoplus}_{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\oplus d}}S_{\infty}\cdot t_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots t_{d}^{a_{d}}.

(4.2)

The corresponding
R
-algebras are

R_{m}:=(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}R^{\square}_{m}\text{ and }R_{\infty}:=(\varinjlim R_{m})^{\wedge}\cong R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R_{\infty}^{\square}.

(4.3)

As a consequence of Lemma
4.1
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.2
.

R_{\infty}
is a torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-module.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.1
(together with
[
GR03
, Lemma 7.1.6(i)]
), it follows that
R_{\infty}^{\square}
is a torsion free
\mathcal{O}
-module. Since
R
is a
p
-completely étale
R^{\square}
-algebra, it follows that
R
is the
p
-adic completion of an étale
R^{\square}
-algebra
R^{\prime}
. Therefore
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R^{\square}}R_{\infty}^{\square}
is a flat
R_{\infty}^{\square}
-algebra. Hence,
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R^{\square}}R_{\infty}^{\square}
is a flat
\mathcal{O}
-algebra. One then applies
[
GR03
, Lemma 7.1.6(i)]
to conclude.
∎

We collect some properties about the algebras
R_{m}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

For all
m\geq 0
,
R_{m}
is
p
-adically complete and
R_{m}
is integrally closed in
R_{m}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
.

Proof.

The first part follows the same idea as the proof of Corollary
4.2
and in fact we use the algebra
R^{\prime}
appearing there. Indeed
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R^{\square}}R_{0}^{\square}
is a flat
R_{0}^{\square}
-algebra. Hence by
[
GR03
, Lemma 7.1.6(i)]
, so is
R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0}
. As
R^{\square}_{m}
is a finitely presented
R^{\square}_{0}
-module, the result follows
[
GR03
, Lemma 7.1.6(ii)]
. The second part follows from
[
Sta18
, Tag 03GG]
(by working with uncompleted algebras).
∎

The summands in (
4.2
) with
a_{j}\notin\mathbb{Z}
for some
1\leq j\leq d
comprise an
R^{\square}
-submodule
M^{\square}_{\infty}
of
R^{\square}_{\infty}
and we set
M_{\infty}:=R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}M_{\infty}^{\square}
. Thus, we have the
R^{\square}
-module (resp.
R
-module) decomposition

R_{\infty}^{\square}\cong(R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\oplus M_{\infty}^{\square}\text{ (resp. }R_{\infty}\cong(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\oplus M_{\infty}\text{)}.

(4.4)

The profinite group

\Delta:=\left\{(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{d})\in\varprojlim_{m\geq 0}\left(\mu_{p^{m}}(\mathcal{O})\right)^{\oplus d}\right\}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus d}

acts
(R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})
-linearly on
R_{m}^{\square}
by scaling each
t_{j}^{1/p^{m}}
by the
\mu_{p^{m}}
-component of
\epsilon_{j}
. The induced actions of
\Delta
on
R_{\infty}^{\square}
and
R_{\infty}
are continuous, compatible, and preserve the decompositions (
4.2
) and (
4.4
).

After inverting
p
, for each
m\geq 0
, we have

R^{\square}_{m}[\tfrac{1}{p}]\cong\bigoplus_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{d}\in\{0,\tfrac{1}{p^{m}},\ldots,\tfrac{p^{m}-1}{p^{m}}\}}(R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\tfrac{1}{p}]\cdot t_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots t_{d}^{a_{d}}

so
R^{\square}_{m}[\frac{1}{p}]
is the
(R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\tfrac{1}{p}]
-algebra obtained by adjoining the
(p^{m})^{\text{th}}
roots of
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\in\left((R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\tfrac{1}{p}]\right)^{\times}
, and hence is finite étale over
(R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. Therefore,
\varinjlim_{m}\left(R_{m}^{\square}[\tfrac{1}{p}]\right)
is a pro-(finite étale)
\Delta
-cover of
(R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. We have
R_{m}^{\square}=\left(R_{m}^{\square}[\tfrac{1}{p}]\right)^{\circ}
, so the pro-object

[
9
9

9

Strictly speaking, to get an honest pro-étale presentation, one should replace each
\Spa(R_{m}^{\square}[\tfrac{1}{p}],R_{m}^{\square})
by the pro-object
X_{m}^{\square}\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}
where
X_{m}^{\square}\to X^{\square}
is a finite étale Kummer cover obtained by adjoining the
(p^{m})^{\text{th}}
roots of
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\in R^{\square}
.
]

X_{\infty}^{\square}:=\varprojlim_{m}\Spa(R_{m}^{\square}[\tfrac{1}{p}],R_{m}^{\square})

is a pro-(finite étale)
\Delta
-cover of the pro-object
X^{\square}\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}
. Of course,
X_{\infty}^{\square}
is an affinoid perfectoid object in the site
X^{\square}_{\proet}
. Base changing the situation along
X\to X^{\square}
we obtain the tower
10
10

10

Analogous to the previous footnote, one should consider the finite étale Kummer cover
X_{m}\to X
to obtain an honest pro-étale presentation.

X_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{m}\Spa(R_{m}[\tfrac{1}{p}],R_{m})

which is a pro-(finite étale)
\Delta
-cover of the pro-object
X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}
. As before
X_{\infty}
becomes an affinoid perfectoid object in the site
X_{\proet}
.

We end this section with a result that allows us to control powerbounded elements in the ring of functions of
X_{\infty}
(or rather the adic space associated to
X_{\infty}
).

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose that
S
is topologically of finite type
over
\mathcal{O}
. Then
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
.

Proof.

We have
R_{\infty}\cong R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R_{\infty}^{\square}
and the completed direct sum decomposition (
4.2
) shows that
R_{\infty}^{\square}
has a
p
-adically completed direct sum decomposition into free
R^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}S_{\infty}
-modules. Therefore
R_{\infty}
has a
p
-adically completed direct sum decomposition into free
R\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}S_{\infty}
-modules. Since
S
is topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, so is
R
. Moreover
R
is topologically of finite presentation by
[
Bos14
, §7.3, Corollary 5]
and therefore
R
is the
p
-adic completion of a free
\mathcal{O}
-module by
[
BMS18
, Lemma 8.10]
(cf. footnote
6
). The result now follows from Lemma
2.13
.
∎

4.2
The cohomology of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
and continuous group cohomology

We prove a relative version of
[
BMS18
, Corollary 8.13(i)]
. By
[
Sch13
, Proposition 3.5, Proposition 3.7(iii), Corollary 6.6]
the Čech complex of the sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
with respect to the pro-(finite étale) affinoid perfectoid cover

X_{\infty}\to X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}

is identified with the continuous cochain complex
R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})
. By
[
Sta18
, Tag 01GY]
this gives an edge map

e\colon R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}).

(4.5)

By the almost purity theorem
[
Sch13
, Lemma 4.10(v)]
, the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathcal{O}
kills the cohomology groups of
\text{Cone}(e)
.
Given an injective resolution
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}\to\mathcal{I}^{\bullet}
, we have

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})

\displaystyle\text{ is represented by }\Gamma(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\mathcal{I}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle Rf_{\eta*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}

\displaystyle\text{ is represented by }f_{\eta*}\mathcal{I}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})

\displaystyle\text{ is represented by }\Gamma(Y_{\infty},f_{\eta*}\mathcal{I}^{\bullet}).

The second and third points follow from
[
Sta18
, Tag 0731]
and
[
Sta18
, Tag 072Z]
when viewing
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
as an object of
D(X_{\text{proét}},\mathcal{O})
or
D(X_{\text{proét}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})
. By definition of the pushforward we get that
\Gamma(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\mathcal{I}^{\bullet})=\Gamma(Y_{\infty},f_{\eta*}\mathcal{I}^{\bullet})
. Therefore the edge map can be rewritten as

e\colon R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}),

(4.6)

viewed as a morphism in
D(\mathcal{O})
.

We will now show that
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e)
is an isomorphism, so that
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}R\Gamma(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})
is computed in terms of continuous group cohomology. For this, we will use
[
BMS18
, Lemma 8.11(i)]
(cf.
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.4]
). In preparation, first we need to control the
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion in the group cohomology of
R_{\infty}/x
for some
x\in\mathcal{O}
.

Proposition 4.5
.

The element
\zeta_{p}-1
kills the
\mathcal{O}
-modules
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,M_{\infty})
. If moreover
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
, then for each
b\in\mathcal{O}
, the
\mathcal{O}
-modules
R_{\infty}/b
and
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}/b)
have no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion.

Proof.

Firstly by Lemma
2.11
, it follows that
R_{\infty}/b
has no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion. We will now show that
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}/b)
has no non-zero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion. As we are in the smooth case, we will have access to a decomposition of
M_{\infty}
of which all of the terms will be
R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R_{0}^{\square}
-modules of rank 1. This will allow us to treat each term one by one, analogous to the situation in
[
BMS18
, Proposition 8.9]
. The semistable case handled in
[
CK19
, Proposition 3.8]
is slightly more delicate. With this in mind consider
S_{\infty}\cdot t_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots t_{d}^{a_{d}}
a summand of (
4.2
) and set
T:=R_{0}^{\square}\cdot t_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots t_{d}^{a_{d}}
.

By
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.7]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.3(ii)]
) the
\mathcal{O}
-module
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,T/b)
is the
i
-th cohomology of the
R_{0}^{\square}/b
-tensor product of
d
-complexes of the form
R_{0}^{\square}/b\xrightarrow{\zeta-1}R_{0}^{\square}/b
for suitable
p
-power roots of unity
\zeta
. By Lemma
4.1
, one obtains

H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,T/b)=M\otimes_{D}R_{0}^{\square},

(4.7)

where
M
is a
D
-module for some discrete valuation ring
D\subset\mathcal{O}
(the
b
is absorbed into the
D
-module
M
). Consider the decomposition

R_{\infty}/b\cong(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/b\oplus\underbrace{\widehat{\bigoplus}_{T}((R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}T)/b}_{M_{\infty}/b}.

(4.8)

where the direct sum runs over those
T
which contribute to
M_{\infty}
. In order to show that
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}/b)
has no non-zero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion, we need to show that both
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/b)
and
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,M_{\infty}/b)
have no non-zero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion.

Since
R
is
R^{\square}
-flat,
[
GR03
, Lemma 7.1.6(i)]
implies
R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0}
is
R^{\square}_{0}
-flat. Moreover
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}T
is
p
-adically complete and so

[
CK19
, Lemma 3.7]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.3(ii)]
) gives

H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,((R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}T)/b)\cong(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,T/b).

(4.9)

Therefore by (
4.7
) and
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.6]
, to show that
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,M_{\infty}/b)
has no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion, it suffices to show that
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}(M\otimes_{D}R_{0}^{\square})
has no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion. This reduces to showing that
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})\otimes_{R^{\square}_{0}}R^{\square}_{0}/d\cong(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})/d
has no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion for some
d\in D
. However

(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})/d\cong(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{d}^{\pm 1}\}/d

and since
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/d
sits inside
R_{\infty}/d
, it follows that
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\square}}R^{\square}_{0})/d
has no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion. Finally
\Delta
acts trivially on
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/b
and so
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/b)
has no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion.

It remains to show
\zeta_{p}-1
kills the
\mathcal{O}
-modules
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,M_{\infty})
. If
T
contributes to
M_{\infty}
, then as in the proof of
[
CK19
, Proposition 3.8]
, we conclude

\zeta_{p}-1\text{ kills }H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,T).

(4.10)

By (
4.8
)-(
4.9
) and
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.6]
, we get the desired result.
∎

Theorem 4.6
.

The edge map
e
defined in (
4.6
) induces an almost isomorphism

L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e)\colon L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})\rightarrow L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}R\Gamma_{\proet}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}).

Similarly the induced map (coming from the inclusion
i\colon R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\hookrightarrow R_{\infty}
)

L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e\circ R\Gamma_{\cont}(i))\colon L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\rightarrow L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}R\Gamma_{\proet}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})

(4.11)

is an almost isomorphism. Moreover if
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
, then both almost isomorphisms are isomorphisms.

Proof.

Recall by §
4.2
, the edge map
e
is an almost isomorphism. Therefore
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e)
is an almost isomorphism. By Proposition
4.5
,
i
induces an isomorphism

\frac{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})}{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})[\zeta_{p}-1]}\cong\frac{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})}{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\zeta_{p}-1]}.

Therefore
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\Gamma_{\cont}(i))
is an isomorphism and hence
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e\circ R\Gamma_{\cont}(i))
is an almost isomorphism.

Suppose now
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
. To show that
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e)
is an isomorphism, it is enough to show that
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e\circ R\Gamma_{\cont}(i))
is an ismorphism. We will apply
[
BMS18
, Lemma 8.11(i)]
(cf.
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.4]
). Proposition
4.5
ensures that the
\mathcal{O}
-modules
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})
have no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion and that the quotient
\tfrac{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})}{(\zeta_{p}-1)H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})}
is a direct sum of
\mathcal{O}
-modules of the form
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/(\zeta_{p}-1)
. These also have no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}
-torsion. Therefore
L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(e\circ R\Gamma_{\cont}(i))
is an isomorphism.
∎

The main goal of this section is an analogue of Theorem
4.6
for the complex
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
(see Theorem
4.11
). To prepare for it, in §
4.3
and §
4.4
we describe the values of the sheaves
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X}
and
W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X})
.

4.3
The tilt
R_{\infty}^{\flat}

We fix a system of compatible
p^{n}
-power roots
p^{1/p^{\infty}}:=(p^{1/p^{n}})_{n\geq 0}
of
p
in
\mathcal{O}
. Thanks to the explicit description (
4.2
) of the perfectoid ring
R_{\infty}^{\square}
, its tilt
(R_{\infty}^{\square})^{\flat}:=\varprojlim_{y\mapsto y^{p}}(R_{\infty}^{\square}/p)
is described explictly by the identification

\displaystyle(R_{\infty}^{\square})^{\flat}

\displaystyle\cong(\varinjlim_{m}S_{\infty}^{\flat}[x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{m}},\ldots,x_{d}^{\pm 1/p^{m}}])^{\wedge}

\displaystyle\cong\widehat{\bigoplus}_{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\oplus d}}S_{\infty}^{\flat}\cdot x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{d}^{a_{d}},

where
x_{i}^{1/p^{m}}
corresponds to the
p
-power compatible sequence
(\ldots,t_{i}^{1/p^{m+1}},t_{i}^{1/p^{m}})
of elements of
R_{\infty}^{\square}
, where the completions are
p^{1/p^{\infty}}
-adic, and the decomposition is as
S_{\infty}^{\flat}
-modules. Thus,

\text{the tilt }R_{\infty}^{\flat}:=\varprojlim_{y\mapsto y^{p}}(R_{\infty}/p)\text{ of the perfectoid ring }R_{\infty}

is identified with the
p^{1/p^{\infty}}
-adic completion of any lift of the étale
R_{\infty}^{\square}/p
-algebra
R_{\infty}/p
to an étale
(R_{\infty}^{\square})^{\flat}
-algebra (such a lift exists by
[
Sta18
, Tag 04D1]
). By
[
Sch13
, Lemma 5.11(i)]
the value on
X_{\infty}
of the sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X}
is the ring
R_{\infty}^{\flat}
.

By functoriality, the group
\Delta
acts continuously and
S_{\infty}^{\flat}
-linearly on
(R_{\infty}^{\square})^{\flat}
and
R_{\infty}^{\flat}
. Explicitly,
\Delta
respects the completed direct sum decomposition and an
(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{d})\in\Delta
scales
x_{j}^{a_{j}}
by
\epsilon_{j}^{a_{j}}\in\mathcal{O}^{\flat}
.

We will need the following analogue of
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.12]
for our analysis in §
4.4
of the value on
X_{\infty}
of the sheaf
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Assume that
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
. Then both
R_{\infty}^{\flat}/b
and
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}^{\flat}/b)
for each
b\in\mathcal{O}^{\flat}\backslash\{0\}
have no nonzero
\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}
-torsion.

Proof.

By using Frobenius we can assume that
b\text{ }\lvert\text{ }p^{1/p^{\infty}}
in
\mathcal{O}^{\flat}
. Then Proposition
4.5
and the
\Delta
-isomorphism
R_{\infty}^{\flat}/b\cong R_{\infty}/b^{\sharp}
for some
b^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{O}
gives the claim.
∎

4.4
The ring
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})

By
[
Sch13
, Theorem 6.5(i)]
, the value of
H^{0}(R\Gamma(X_{\infty},\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}))
is the ring

\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}):=W(R_{\infty}^{\flat}).

In general we will denote by
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(P)
for a perfectoid ring
P
over
\mathcal{O}
to be
W(P^{\flat})
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Assume that
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
. Then each quotient

\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/(p^{n},\mu^{n^{\prime}})\text{ so also }\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/\mu\text{ has no nonzero }W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})\text{-torsion}.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.7
(cf.
[
CK19
, (3.14.1)]
).
∎

Due to the explicit construction of Witt vectors we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})

\displaystyle\cong(\varinjlim_{m}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})[X_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{m}},\ldots,X_{d}^{\pm 1/p^{m}}])^{\wedge}

\displaystyle\cong\widehat{\bigoplus}_{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{p}]^{\oplus d}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})\cdot X_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots X_{d}^{a_{d}},

where the completions are
(p,\mu)
-adic, the decomposition is as
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
-modules and in terms of the notation in §
4.3
,
X_{i}^{1/p^{m}}=[x_{i}^{1/p^{m}}]
. The summands for which
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
i
comprise a subring

A(R^{\square})=\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})\{X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{d}^{\pm 1}\}\text{ inside }\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})

where the convergence is
(p,\mu)
-adic. The remaining summands, that is, those for which
a_{i}\notin\mathbb{Z}
comprise a
A(R^{\square})
-submodule
N^{\square}_{\infty}\subset\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})
.

There is a canonical surjective ring homomorphism

\theta\colon\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})\twoheadrightarrow R_{\infty}^{\square}

whose kernel is a principal ideal, cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 3.10]
, such that

\theta\lvert_{A(R^{\square})}\colon A(R^{\square})\twoheadrightarrow R^{\square}\text{ is described by }X_{i}\mapsto t_{i}.

(4.12)

The
p
-completely étale map
R^{\square}\to R
deforms uniquely (along (
4.12
)) to a
(p,\mu)
-completely étale map
A(R^{\square})\to A(R)
, where
A(R)
is
(p,\mu)
-adically complete. By construction, we have the identification

\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})\cong\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})\widehat{\otimes}_{A(R^{\square})}A(R),

(4.13)

where the completion is
(p,\mu)
-adic. Therefore, by setting
N_{\infty}:=N_{\infty}^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}_{A(R^{\square})}A(R)
, we arrive at the decompositions of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})
and
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
into “integral” and “nonintegral” parts:

\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})\cong A(R^{\square})\oplus N_{\infty}^{\square}\text{ and }\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})\cong A(R)\oplus N_{\infty}.

(4.14)

Modulo
\xi
, these decompositions reduce to the decompositions in (
4.4
).

The Witt vector Frobenius of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}^{\square})
preserves
A(R^{\square})
; explictly: it is semilinear with respect to the Frobenius of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
and raises each
X_{i}^{1/p^{m}}
to the
p
-th power. By construction,
A(R)
inherits a Frobenius ring endomorphism from
A(R^{\square})
, and the identification (
4.13
) is Frobenius-equivariant.

The natural
\Delta
-action on
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
is continuous and commutes with the Frobenius. Explictly,
\Delta
respects the completed direct sum decomposition (
4.14
) and an
(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{d})\in\Delta
scales
X_{j}^{a_{j}}
by
[\epsilon_{j}^{a_{j}}]\in A_{\Inf}
. The
\Delta
-action on
A(R^{\square})
lifts uniquely to a necessarily Frobenius-equivariant
\Delta
-action on
A(R)
, which is trivial on the quotient
A(R)\to R
. In particular,
\Delta
acts trivially on
A(R)/\mu
. The identifications (
4.13
)-(
4.14
) are
\Delta
-equivariant.

4.5
The cohomology of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
and continuous group cohomology

Similarly to §
4.2
, the Čech complex of the sheaf
W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X})
with respect to the pro-(finite étale) affinoid perfectoid cover

X_{\infty}\to X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}

is identified with the continuous cochain complex
R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))
. Thus, by
[
Sta18
, Tag 01GY]
, we obtain the map to the pro-étale cohomology of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
(obtained by composing the edge map with
R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X}))\to R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})
):

e\colon R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\text{proét}}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}).

(4.15)

By the almost purity theorem, more precisely, by
[
Sch13
, Theorem 6.5(ii)]
, the subset
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]\subset A_{\Inf}
that consists of the Teichmüller lifts of the elements in the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}\subset\mathcal{O}_{C^{\flat}}
kills all the cohomology groups of
\text{Cone}(e)
. In fact using
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.17]
and the fact that pushforwards preserve derived
p
-completions (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
), it follows that each
H^{i}(\text{Cone}(e))
is killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
.

Proposition 4.9
.

For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
A_{\Inf}
-module
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/\mu)
is
p
-torsion free and
p
-adically complete; moreover, the following natural maps are isomorphisms:

H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/\mu)\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/p^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/(\mu,p^{n}))\text{ for }n>0

(4.16)

and

H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/\mu)\xrightarrow{\sim}\varprojlim_{n}H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/(\mu,p^{n})).

(4.17)

In addition, if
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
, then
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/(\mu,p^{n}))
and
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/\mu)
have no nonzero
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
-torsion.

Proof.

The first step is to use the decomposition (
4.14
) to reduce proving the proposition for the “nonintegral” part of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
. Since
A(R)/\mu
is
p
-adically complete and has a trivial
\Delta
-action (see
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.13]
and §
4.4
),
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.7]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.3(ii)]
) implies that
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,A(R)/\mu)
is a direct sum of copies of
A(R)/\mu
and likewise for
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,A(R)/(\mu,p^{n}))
. Furthermore, if
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
then Lemma
4.8
implies that the rings
A(R)/\mu
and
A(R)/(\mu,p^{n})
have no nonzero
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
-torsion. Thus we only need to establish all claims with
N_{\infty}
in place of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
.

We now reduce the situation to that of
[
CK19
, Proposition 3.19]
. For this we will need to keep track of the
S_{\infty}
appearing in the definition of
N_{\infty}^{\square}
. Therefore it will be convenient to temporarily denote
N_{\infty}^{\square}
by
N_{\infty}^{\square}(S_{\infty})
. The identifications

\displaystyle N_{\infty}^{\square}(S_{\infty})/(\mu,p^{n})

\displaystyle\cong N_{\infty}^{\square}(\mathcal{O})/(\mu,p^{n})\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})\text{ for }n>0

\displaystyle N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n})

\displaystyle\cong N_{\infty}^{\square}(S_{\infty})/(\mu,p^{n})\otimes_{A(R^{\square})}A(R)

are
\Delta
-equivariant. Furthermore
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/(\mu,p^{n})
is
A_{\Inf}/(\mu,p^{n})
-flat (cf. Lemma 3.13 in loc.cit.) and
A(R)/(\mu,p^{n})
is
A(R^{\square})/(\mu,p^{n})
-flat (because
A(R)
is a
(\mu,p)
-completely étale
A(R^{\square})
-algebra). Thus the short exact sequence
[
CK19
, (3.19.9)]
established, in particular, for
N_{\infty}^{\square}(\mathcal{O})
holds also for
N_{\infty}
:

0\to H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))[p]\to H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))\to H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n-1}))\to 0,

(4.18)

for
n>1
. The transition morphisms
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))\to H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n-1}))
are in particular surjective and so
R^{1}\varprojlim_{n}H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))
vanishes. Therefore by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0D6K]
, we obtain

H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/\mu)\xrightarrow{\sim}\varprojlim_{n}H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n})),

(4.19)

which is the sought analogue of (
4.17
). The
p
-torsion freeness of
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/\mu)
follows from (
4.18
)-(
4.19
). Therefore by
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.7]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.3(ii)]
) and
[
Sta18
, Tag 061Z]
, we obtain

H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/\mu)\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/p^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))\text{ for }n>0,

which is the sought analogue of (
4.16
).

It remains to show that each
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))
has no nonzero
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
-torsion, under the assumption
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
. The short exact sequence (
4.18
) shows that
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p^{n}))
is a successive extension of copies of
H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,N_{\infty}/(\mu,p))
. Now
N_{\infty}/(\mu,p)
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/(\mu,p)\cong R_{\infty}^{\flat}/\mu
. The result now follows from Lemma
4.7
.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

A remark about the proof of Proposition
4.9
. We have

A(R^{\square})=\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})\{X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{d}^{\pm 1}\}\cong A_{\Inf}\{X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{d}^{\pm 1}\}\widehat{\otimes}_{A_{\Inf}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),

where the completion is
(p,\mu)
-adic. Thus it would be natural to attempt to give a more direct reduction of Proposition
4.9
to
[
CK19
, Proposition 3.19]
where the statement is proved, in particular, for the ring
A_{\Inf}\{X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{d}^{\pm 1}\}
. However we do not know whether
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
is a flat
A_{\Inf}
-algebra and it is unclear how to bring the completed tensor product outside the cohomology groups.

Theorem 4.11
.

The edge map defined in (
4.15
) induces a morphism

L\eta_{\mu}(e)\colon L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\to L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\proet}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})

such that the cohomology groups of
\text{Cone}(L\eta_{\mu}(e))
are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})\subset A_{\Inf}
.
Moreover if
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
, then this morphism is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Recall by §
4.5
, the cohomology groups of
\text{Cone}(e)
are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
. Therefore
L\eta_{\mu}(e)
satisfies the analogous claim.

Suppose now
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
. By the projection formula
[
Sta18
, Tag 0944]
,

R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}/\mu\cong R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})/\mu).

(4.20)

Therefore by Proposition
4.9
, the cohomology modules of
R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}/\mu
have no nonzero
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
-torsion. Thus the claim follows from
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.18]
.
∎

Later when we will compare
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
with
q
-crystalline cohomology (cf. Theorem
6.28
) we will need to vary
\Delta
in Theorem
4.11
to accommodate for the “all possible coordinates” technique. The following remark will make this possible (cf.
[
CK19
, Remark 3.21]
).

Remark 4.12
.

For any profinite group
\Delta^{\prime}
equipped with a continuous surjection
\Delta^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\Delta
and any pro-(finite étale) affinoid perfectoid
\Delta^{\prime}
-cover

\Spa(R_{\infty}^{\prime}[\tfrac{1}{p}],R^{\prime}_{\infty})\to X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}\hskip 5.69054pt\text{ that refines the $\Delta$-cover }\hskip 5.69054ptX_{\infty}\to X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}

compatibly with the surjection
\Delta^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\Delta
, the edge map
e^{\prime}
defined analogously to (
4.15
) induces a morphism

L\eta_{\mu}(e^{\prime})\colon L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta^{\prime},\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R^{\prime}_{\infty}))\to L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\proet}(Y_{\infty},Rf_{\eta*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})

which satisfies the same conditions as in Theorem
4.11
. The same line of reasoning as in loc.cit. applies. In general by the octahedral axiom the morphism

L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R^{\prime}_{\infty}))\to L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta^{\prime},\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R^{\prime}_{\infty}))

(4.21)

has cone whose cohomology groups are killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
and therefore by
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.17]
, it is also killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
. If in addition
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
, then (
4.21
) is an isomorphism.

5
The Hodge-Tate specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

In this section we assume that
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b) over
\mathcal{O}
, as in
[
Hub94
, §1.9]
, and
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is a smooth morphism. To state the results in this section, we will often have to resort to using the language of almost
\mathcal{O}
-sheaves. This is particularly the case, outside of some finite type assumptions. For this we refer the reader to §
2.3
, and in particular the functor (cf. Definition
2.24
)

\displaystyle D(\mathscr{C},\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\to D(\mathscr{C},\mathcal{O}^{a})

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{F}^{a}.

With the local analysis of §
4
at our disposal, we turn to the analysis of the Hodge-Tate specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
. Analogous to
[
BMS18
, §9.2]
, we consider a presheaf version of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
.

5.1
The presheaf version
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}

We begin this section with a result that good objects in
D
(cf. Definition
2.9
) are, under some mild conditions, sufficiently well behaved. Following the notation of §
2.1
and
4.1
, for a good object

(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))

(5.1)

in
D
, one has the associated rings
S_{\infty}
and
R_{\infty}
(cf. (
4.3
)).

Lemma 5.1
.

If
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then for every good object (cf. (
5.1
)), one has
(R_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}])^{\circ}=R_{\infty}
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
4.4
.
∎

In addition to the site
D
constructed in §
2.1
, we consider the site
D^{\text{psh}}
, whose objects are good objects in
D
, and coverings are isomorphisms. In this way, every presheaf is already a sheaf. We denote the associated topos by
(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et})^{\text{psh}}
. There is a morphism of topoi

(\phi^{-1},\phi_{*})\colon Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\to(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et})^{\text{psh}}

for which
\phi_{*}
is given by restricting sheaves on
D
to
D^{\text{psh}}
and
\phi^{-1}
is given by sheafification. In particular, since any sheaf is the sheafification of its associated presheaf,
\phi^{-1}\circ\phi_{*}\cong\id
. We let

\nu_{f}^{\text{psh}}:=\phi\circ\nu_{f}\colon X_{\proet}\to(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et})^{\text{psh}}

be the indicated composition of morphisms of topoi (with
\nu_{f}
defined in (
2.2
)) and set

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}:=L\eta_{\mu}(R\nu_{f*}^{\text{psh}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})\in D^{\geq 0}(D^{\text{psh}},A_{\Inf}).

Since
L\eta
commutes with pullback under flat morphisms of ringed topoi (cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.14]
)

\phi^{-1}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})\cong A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}.

(5.2)

Moreover, again by
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.14]
,
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
may be described explictly: for every object
Z=(W\to\mathfrak{U}\leftarrow\mathfrak{V})
of
D^{\text{psh}}
, we have (cf. (
2.3
))

R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})=L\eta_{\mu}(R\Gamma(W\times_{U}V,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})).

(5.3)

In particular, since, by
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.19]
, the functor
L\eta_{\mu}
preserves derived completeness when used in the context of a
replete
topos (such as the topos of sets), we see from (
5.3
) that
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
is derived
\xi
-adically (and also
\tilde{\xi}
-adically) complete (cf. Lemma
2.27
). Armed with the formalism of §
5.1
, we now identify the Hodge-Tate specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
.

Theorem 5.2
.

There exists a morphism in
D(D,\mathcal{O})

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\to L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})),

(5.4)

whose image in
D(D,\mathcal{O}^{a})
is an almost isomorphism. If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
then (
5.4
) is already an isomorphism in
D(D,\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

We will employ the language of §
2.3
. Let
\theta_{X}\colon W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X})\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
be the natural map so that the kernel of
\theta_{X}\circ\varphi^{-1}\colon W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X})\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
is generated by the nonzero divisor
\tilde{\xi}
. The projection formula (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0944]
) provides the identification

R\nu_{f*}(W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\cong R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}).

(5.5)

By
[
Zav21
, Lemma C.11]

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
is derived
p
-adically complete and so by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
, the RHS of (
5.5
) is derived
p
-adically complete. Thus there is no harm in replacing
W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{X})
appearing on the LHS of (
5.5
) with it’s derived
p
-adic completion to obtain:

R\nu_{f*}(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\cong R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}).

Since
(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1})(\mu)=\zeta_{p}-1
, this induces the map (
5.4
) and, likewise also its presheaf version

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\to L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\phi_{*}(R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))).

(5.6)

Due to (
5.2
),
\phi^{-1}
brings (
5.6
) to (
5.4
), so by Proposition
2.25
, it suffices to show that (
5.6
) is an almost isomorphism (if
\mathfrak{Y}
is in addition locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, it suffices to show (
5.6
) is an isomorphism).

Consider an object
Z=(W\to\mathfrak{U}\leftarrow\mathfrak{V})=(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))
of
D^{\text{psh}}
. The discussion and notation of §
4
apply. In particular, Proposition
4.9
and (
4.20
) ensure that the cohomology of
R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}/\mu
is
p
-torsion free. Thus, since
\tilde{\xi}\equiv p\pmod{\mu}
,
[
Bha18
, Lemma 5.16]
implies that

L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}\xrightarrow{\sim}L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})).

(5.7)

Now Theorem
4.11
gives a morphism

L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\to L\eta_{\mu}(R\Gamma(W\times_{U}V,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}

(5.8)

whose cone has cohomology groups killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})\otimes_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\cong\mathfrak{m}
(cf. Lemma
2.26
).
Similarly Theorem
4.6
gives a morphism

L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}))\to L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\Gamma(W\times_{U}V,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))

(5.9)

whose cone has cohomology groups killed by
\mathfrak{m}
. Since the edge maps (
4.5
) and (
4.15
) are compatible (
5.7
)-(
5.9
) give the desired conclusion (if
\mathfrak{Y}
is in addition locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then by Lemma
5.1
, (
5.8
)-(
5.9
) are isomorphisms).
∎

5.2
The completed integral structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}

In this section, we compute the (local) sections of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}
for good objects, cf. Proposition
5.7
. The strategy is as follows

(1)

Compute the sections of the pushforward
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
for good objects (cf. Lemma
5.3
).

(2)

Show
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
is
p
-adically complete (cf. Lemma
5.5
(iv)).

(3)

Compare
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
with
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For a good object

Z=(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R)),

we have
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z)=R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
.

Proof.

We employ the notation of §
4.1
. By (
4.4
), recall that we have a decomposition

R_{\infty}\cong(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\oplus M_{\infty}

and since
M_{\infty}^{\Delta}
is killed by
(\zeta_{p}-1)
(cf. Proposition
4.5
), we immediately obtain by Corollary
4.2
,

R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\cong H^{0}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}).

The edge map (
4.5
) is an isomorphism in degree 0. Thus we a get a natural isomorphism (in
R
,
S
and
S_{\infty}
)

R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{0}(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}).

(5.10)

By (
2.3
), the RHS is precisely
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z)
.
∎

Lemma
5.3
computes the sections of
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
for good objects. We now begin our comparison of
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
with
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}
. This will involve showing that
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
is
p
-adically complete (as a sheaf). The following (slight) strengthening of the proof of
[
Sch12
, Proposition 7.13]
will be useful.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
U_{\infty}
be an affinoid perfectoid space over
\Spa(C,\mathcal{O})
. Then for any covering
\{U_{i}\to U_{\infty}\}
(with each
U_{i}
affinoid) in
U_{\infty,\et}
(in the sense of
[
Sch12
, Definition 7.1(iii)]
), the complex

0\to\mathcal{O}_{U_{\infty}}(U_{\infty})^{\circ a}\to\prod_{i}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}(U_{i})^{\circ a}\to\prod_{i,j}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}\times_{U_{\infty}}U_{j}}(U_{i}\times_{U_{\infty}}U_{j})^{\circ a}\to\ldots

is exact.

Proof.

The statement is proved in the proof of
[
Sch12
, Proposition 7.13]
where in addition the covering is finite and each
U_{i}\to U_{\infty}
is a composition of rational embeddings and finite étale maps. To obtain the general statement one takes a finite refinement
\{V_{j}\to U_{\infty}\}
of
\{U_{i}\to U_{\infty}\}
such that each
V_{j}\to U_{\infty}
is a composition of rational embeddings and finite étale maps. The key point is that the Čech complex associated to the covering of
\{V_{j}\to U_{\infty}\}
restricted to each
U_{i_{1}}\times_{U_{\infty}}\ldots\times_{U_{\infty}}U_{i_{p}}
is almost exact (again by the proof in loc.cit.). By the same argument as
[
BGR84
, §8.1.4, Corollary 3]
, this implies that the augmented Čech complex associated to
\{V_{j}\to U_{\infty}\}
is almost exact iff the same is true for
\{U_{i}\to U_{\infty}\}
11
11

11

For the corresponding argument
outside
of the almost setting, cf.
[
KL15
, Proposition 8.2.21]
.
.
∎

We now show an analogue of
[
Sch13
, Lemma 4.10]
for
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
Z=(W\to\mathfrak{U}\leftarrow\mathfrak{V})=(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))\in D
be a good object. In the following, we use the almost setting w.r.t.
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathcal{O}
.

(1)

We have
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z)/p^{n}=(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty})/p^{n}
, and this is almost equal to
((\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n})(Z)
.

(2)

The cohomology groups
H^{i}(Z,(\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n})
are almost zero for
i>0
and
n\geq 1
.

(3)

The image of
((\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n+1})(Z)
in
((\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n})(Z)
is
(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty})/p^{n}
for all
n\geq 1
.

(4)

The sheaf
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
is
p
-adically complete:
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}=\varprojlim(\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n}
.

(5)

The cohomology groups
H^{i}(Z,\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})
are almost zero for
i>0
.

Proof.

The equality
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z)/p^{n}=(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty})/p^{n}
follows from Lemma
5.3
. For parts (i)-(ii), by Proposition
2.25
and the exactness of
\widetilde{\alpha}\colon\Mod_{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}})\to\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{a}
(cf. §
2.3
)
it suffices to show

Z\mapsto\mathscr{F}(Z):=(\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z)/p^{n})^{a}

is a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}^{a}
-modules and
H^{i}(Z,\mathscr{F})=0
for
i>0
.

Let
Z
be covered by
Z_{k}\to Z
, where
Z_{k}
are objects
as in Proposition
2.7
(1). By Proposition
2.7
(2)
Z
is quasi-compact, so we can assume that there are only finitely many
Z_{k}
or a single
T
(by taking the disjoint union componentwise). After taking a refinement we can assume
T
is good (cf. Proposition
2.16
(1)), and of the form

T=(\varprojlim W_{j}\to\mathfrak{U}_{0}\leftarrow\mathfrak{V}_{0})

such that

(1)

W_{j}\to W_{j^{\prime}}
is finite étale surjective for
j\geq j^{\prime}\geq 0
.

(2)

\varprojlim W_{j}\to U_{0}
factors as
\varprojlim W_{j}\to W_{0}\to U_{0}
, where
U_{0}
is the adic generic fiber of
\mathfrak{U}_{0}
.

In particular this implies that
W_{j}\times_{U_{0}}V_{0}\to W\times_{U}V
is étale surjective for
j\geq 0
, where
V_{0}
is the adic generic fiber of
\mathfrak{V}_{0}
. It suffices to check that the complex

C(T\to Z,\mathscr{F})\colon 0\to\mathscr{F}(Z)\to\mathscr{F}(T)\to\mathscr{F}(T\times_{Z}T)\to\cdots

(5.11)

is exact. Note that by Lemma
5.3

(1)

\mathscr{F}(Z)=(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty})^{a}/p^{n}
, and

(2)

\mathscr{F}(T)=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(\varprojlim W_{j}\times_{U_{0}}V_{0})^{a}/p^{n}=\varinjlim(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(W_{j}\times_{U_{0}}V_{0}))^{a}/p^{n}
.

Moreover the values of
\mathscr{F}(Z)
and
\mathscr{F}(T)
determine the values of all the terms in (
5.11
) as each
\underbrace{T\times_{Z}\ldots\times_{Z}T}_{\text{$r$ times}}
is good (good objects are stable under fiber products) and

\mathscr{F}(\underbrace{T\times_{Z}\ldots\times_{Z}T}_{\text{$r$ times}})=\underbrace{\mathscr{F}(T)\times_{\mathscr{F}(Z)}\ldots\times_{\mathscr{F}(Z)}\mathscr{F}(T)}_{\text{$r$ times}}.

Now
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
is not quite perfectoid, so the final step is to base change (
5.11
) to a sequence of perfectoid algebras.

Employing the notation in §
4
we have the pro-(finite étale) cover
U_{\infty}\to W\times_{U}V
in
X_{\proet}
. Let
U_{j,\infty}\to U_{j}
be the étale cover determined by
W_{j}\times_{U_{0}}V_{0}\to W\times_{U}V
.

Lemma 5.6
.

The morphism
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\to R_{\infty}
is
p
-completely faithfully flat.

Proof.

Note that each
R_{m}
is nonzero and finite free over
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
and
R_{\infty}
is the (derived)
p
-adic completion of
\varinjlim R_{m}
.
∎

So it suffices to show that the complex in (
5.11
) base changed along
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})^{a}\to R_{\infty}^{a}
:

0\to(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\widehat{U}_{\infty}}(\widehat{U}_{\infty})/p^{n})^{a}\to\varinjlim(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\widehat{U}_{\infty}}(\widehat{U}_{j,\infty})/p^{n})^{a}\to\varinjlim(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\widehat{U}_{\infty}}(\widehat{U}_{j,\infty}\times_{\widehat{U}_{\infty}}\widehat{U}_{j,\infty})/p^{n})^{a}\to\cdots

(5.12)

is exact, where
\widehat{U}_{\infty}
(resp.
\widehat{U}_{j,\infty}
) is the corresponding affinoid perfectoid space associated to
U_{\infty}
(resp.
U_{j,\infty}
). This follows from Lemma
5.4
.

We now show part (iii). Let
f\in((\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n+1})(Z)
. By part (1), there exists
g\in R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
such that
pf=g
in
((\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n+1})(Z)
. This means that there exists a covering
\{Z_{k}\to Z\}
and
f_{k}\in\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z_{k})
such that

pf_{k}=g\text{ in }\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}(Z_{k}).

(5.13)

On the other hand we can write
g=ph
for some
h\in(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. By (
5.13
), we get that
h\in R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
. As multiplication by
p\colon(\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n}\to(\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n+1}
is an injection, we get that
f=h
in
((\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})/p^{n})(Z)
. This completes the proof of part (iii).

Part (iv) is a consequence of part (iii). Finally part (v) is a consequence of parts (ii) and (iv) together with the almost version of
[
Sch13
, Lemma 3.18]
.
∎

We can finally compare
\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
and establish a morphism of ringed topoi.

Proposition 5.7
.

There is a morphism of ringed topoi

(\nu_{f},\nu_{f}^{\sharp})\colon(X_{\proet},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\to(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D})

with
\nu_{f}^{\sharp}\colon\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}\xrightarrow{\sim}\nu_{f^{*}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})
. Moreover for a good object

Z=(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R)),

we have
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)=R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
.

Proof.

By definition

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}:=\varprojlim(q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}\otimes_{p^{-1}\nu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}}p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y})/p^{n}

and so by Lemma
5.5
(iv), it suffices to construct a morphism

q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}\otimes_{p^{-1}\nu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}}p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}\to\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})

(5.14)

which gives an isomorphism modulo
p^{n}
. By definition the LHS of (
5.14
) is sheafification of the presheaf
Z\mapsto\mathscr{F}(Z):=R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty}
. Since
\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})(Z)=R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
which is natural in
R
,
S
and
S_{\infty}
(cf. Lemma
5.3
and its proof), we get a map
\mathscr{F}\to\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})
of presheaves which is an isomorphism modulo
p^{n}
. Sheafifying this map gives the sought after (
5.14
).
∎

Finally we conclude this section by comparing
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
with its derived
p
-adic completion
R\varprojlim(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/p^{n})
:

Lemma 5.8
.

The completed integral structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
is derived
p
-adically complete.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.7
we identify
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
with
\nu_{f^{*}}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})
and use the notation of Lemma
5.5
. By a similar proof to
[
Zav21
, Lemma C.11]
, it suffices to show
R\Gamma(Z,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D})
is derived
p
-adically complete. By
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.15]
, it suffices to show for each
i\geq 0
,
H^{i}(Z,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D})
is derived
p
-adically complete. For
i=0
, this follows from second part of Proposition
5.7
. For
i\geq 1
, this follows from Lemma
5.5
(v).
∎

5.3
The object
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

Armed with §
5.2
, to proceed further, we need to analyze the right side of (
5.4
), namely

\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}:=L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))\in D^{\geq 0}(D,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}).

First we show that the morphism of ringed topoi
(\nu_{f},\nu_{f}^{\sharp})
constructed in Proposition
5.7
satisfies the expected base change:

Lemma 5.9
.

Suppose the morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
sits in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Y},}

(5.15)

where
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{Y}
are étale. Then (
5.15
) induces a commutative diagram of ringed topoi:

\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{\proet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{f^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\et}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{X_{\proet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{f}}
\textstyle{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}

and the base change morphism
j^{*}R^{n}\nu_{f*}\mathscr{F}\to R^{n}\nu_{f^{\prime}*}i^{*}\mathscr{F}

is an isomorphism (here
\mathscr{F}
is an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}
-module).

Proof.

The key point is that if

Z^{\prime}:=(W^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathfrak{V}^{\prime})

is a good object in
Y^{\prime}_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\et}
, then it is also a good object in
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
. The commutativity follows from the functoriality of the edge map (
4.5
) (cf. proof of Lemma
5.3
). For the isomorphism claim, one checks that both sides (of the base change morphism) is sheafification of the presheaf

Z^{\prime}\mapsto H_{\text{proét}}^{n}(V^{\prime}\times_{U^{\prime}}W^{\prime},\mathscr{F}),

where as usual,
U^{\prime}
(resp.
V^{\prime}
) is the adic generic fiber of
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}
).
∎

A basic property is that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
behaves well with respect to base change:

Corollary 5.10
.

In the situation of Lemma
5.9
,
j^{*}\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\cong\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}/\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

The result follows by Lemma
5.9
together with the fact that
L\eta
commutes with flat base change (cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.14]
).
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

We have
H^{0}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
. For
i\geq 1
, the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+a}_{D}
-module
H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})^{a}
is Zariski locally on
\mathfrak{X}
the pullback of a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}^{a}
-module of rank
\binom{\dim_{x}f_{k}^{-1}(f_{k}(x))}{i}
at a variable closed point
x
of
\mathfrak{X}_{k}
. If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then the almost can be dropped.

Proof.

The isomorphism
H^{0}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
is a consequence of Proposition
5.7
. So assume
i\geq 1
.

By Lemma
2.15
and Corollary
5.10
, we can assume that that
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(S)
,
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
and for some
d\geq 0
,
there is an étale
\Spf(S)
-morphism

\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)\rightarrow\Spf(R^{\square})=:\mathfrak{X}^{\square}\text{ with }R^{\square}:=S\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{d}^{\pm 1}\}.

(5.16)

To achieve this, we can first shrink
\mathfrak{X}
(as the statement depends Zariski locally on
\mathfrak{X}
) and then
\mathfrak{Y}
as this does not change the sections of sheaves in the fibered topoi (cf. Lemma
2.3
for coverings of type (c)).
Henceforth the discussion and notation of §
4
apply. In particular, since
R
is
R^{\square}
-flat and
\Delta
acts trivially on
R^{\square}
and
R
,
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.7]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.3(ii)]
) and Proposition
4.5
imply that

(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})^{\oplus\binom{d}{i}}\cong H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R^{\square})\otimes_{R^{\square}}R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\cong\frac{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}^{\square})}{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty}^{\square})[\zeta_{p}-1]}\otimes_{R^{\square}}R\cong\frac{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})}{H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})[\zeta_{p}-1]}.

(5.17)

Theorem
4.6
shows that

(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})^{\oplus\binom{d}{i}}\cong\frac{H^{i}(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})}{H^{i}(X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})[\zeta_{p}-1]}

(5.18)

is an almost isomorphism of free
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
-modules of rank
\binom{d}{i}
. Consequently by Proposition
5.7
we get almost isomorphisms

(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+})^{\oplus\binom{d}{i}}\cong\frac{R^{i}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})}{(R^{i}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))[\zeta_{p}-1]}\cong H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}),

(5.19)

to the effect that
H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
is the pullback of an almost free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}
-module of rank
\binom{d}{i}
as desired. Finally if
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then by Lemma
5.1
, (
5.18
)-(
5.19
) are isomorphisms.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

The proof of Proposition
5.11
, specifically (
5.18
)-(
5.19
), shows that if
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are affine, then the presheaf assigning
\frac{H^{i}(X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y_{\infty}^{\prime},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X^{\prime}})}{H^{i}(X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y_{\infty}^{\prime},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X^{\prime}})[\zeta_{p}-1]}
to a variable good object
(\varprojlim_{j}Y_{j}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathfrak{X}^{\prime})
over
(Y\to\mathfrak{Y}\leftarrow\mathfrak{X})
is an almost
\mathcal{O}
-sheaf (as usual
Y_{\infty}^{\prime}
is the perfectoid affinoid determined by
\varprojlim_{j}Y_{j}^{\prime}
). If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
,
then it is a sheaf, although we will not use this fact.

Almost in the following corollary is w.r.t.
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
.

Corollary 5.13
.

The object
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is almost derived
\xi
-adically complete (i.e. the canonical morphism
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to R\varprojlim_{n}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/\xi^{n})
is an almost isomorphism) and

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}\to R\phi_{*}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\overset{\eqref{eq:pullbackofpregivshe}}{\cong}R\phi_{*}(\phi^{-1}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}))

(5.20)

is an almost isomorphism. If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(\mathcal{O})
and
f
is smooth, then the almost can be dropped.

Proof.

Since
\phi^{-1}\circ R\phi_{*}\cong\id
, to show that
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is almost derived
\xi
-adically complete, it suffices to show that

R\phi_{*}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to R\phi_{*}R\varprojlim_{n}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/\xi^{n})

is an almost isomorphism. Since
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
is derived
\xi
-adically complete (cf. §
5.1
), we reduce to proving (
5.20
). We can assume
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are affine, and there is a framing as in (
5.16
). By the almost analogue of
[
BMS18
, Lemma 9.15]
for the site
D^{\text{psh}}
defined in §
5.1
and the fact that
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
is derived
\tilde{\xi}
-adically complete, it suffices to show

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/\tilde{\xi}^{n}\to R\phi_{*}(\phi^{-1}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/\tilde{\xi}^{n}))

is an almost isomorphism.
By induction on
n
, we may assume
n=1
and in this case by (
5.6
)

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/\tilde{\xi}\to L\eta_{(\zeta_{p}-1)}(R\phi_{*}(R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})))

(5.21)

is an almost isomorphism. Finally by Remark
5.12
, the cohomology presheaves of the RHS of (
5.21
) are in fact almost
\mathcal{O}
-sheaves (note that by Lemma
5.5
(ii),
R^{i}\phi_{*}
of these almost
\mathcal{O}
-sheaves are almost zero for
i>0
). Of course in the case
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(\mathcal{O})
and
f
smooth, then the
R^{i}\phi_{*}
of these almost
\mathcal{O}
-sheaves vanishes for
i>0
, as a geometric point has no higher cohomology groups.
∎

Remark 5.14
.

In general, the “almost” in Corollary
5.13
cannot be dropped even in the case
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
. The issue is that even in this case,
R^{i}\phi_{*}
of the sheaves appearing in Remark
5.12
are only almost zero in general.

Before we continue, we actually have enough at our disposal to compare our objects with the ones constructed in
[
BMS18
]
. So let us take a brief detour to do this. Let
r\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\Zar}
be the natural morphism of topoi and recall the projection morphism
q\colon Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(\mathcal{O})
and
f
is smooth. Then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}}\cong Rr_{*}Rq_{*}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\Spf(\mathcal{O})}

where
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}}:=L\eta_{\mu}(Rr_{*}Rq_{*}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})
as defined in
[
BMS18
, Definition 9.1]
.

Proof.

By
[
CK19
, Corollary 4.21]
it suffices to show that there is a natural quasi-isomorphism

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}\cong Rq_{*}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\Spf(\mathcal{O})},

where
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}:=L\eta_{\mu}(Rq_{*}R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X})
is the main object studied in
[
CK19
]
. Let
q^{\text{psh}}\colon(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et})^{\text{psh}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\et}^{\text{psh}}
denote the presheaf version of
q
. Then by (4.1.3) in loc.cit. and (
5.3
) we get

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}^{\text{psh}}\cong Rq_{*}^{\text{psh}}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\Spf(\mathcal{O})}^{\text{psh}},

where
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}^{\text{psh}}
is the presheaf version of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}
defined in (4.1.1) in loc.cit. However
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}^{\text{psh}}
is already a sheaf by (4.6.1) in loc.cit. It remains to show
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\Spf(\mathcal{O})}^{\text{psh}}
is also a sheaf. This follows from the last statement of Corollary
5.13
.
∎

In the spirit of
[
BMS18
, Theorem 8.3]
and
[
CK19
, Theorem 4.11]
, our next task is to identify
H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
with the twists of the bundles given by (continuous) Kähler differentials. For this, in Proposition
5.16
, we first express
H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
as
\bigwedge^{i}H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
.

5.3.1
The cup product maps

By the same arguments as in
[
Sta18
, Tag 068G]
, there are product maps

R^{j}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}}R^{j^{\prime}}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\xrightarrow{-\cup-}H^{j+j^{\prime}}(R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}}^{\mathbb{L}}R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))

that satisfy
x\cup y=(-1)^{jj^{\prime}}y\cup x
(cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0BYI]
). By
[
Sta18
, Tag 0B6C]
, there is a cup product map

R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}}^{\mathbb{L}}R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\to R\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}).

These maps combine to give the
cup product map
(where the tensor product is over
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}
)

\bigotimes_{s=1}^{i}R^{1}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\to R^{i}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})\text{ for each }i>0

(5.22)

Proposition 5.16
.

For each
i>0
, the map (
5.22
) induces an almost isomorphism

\bigwedge^{i}\left(\frac{R^{1}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})}{(R^{1}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))[\zeta_{p}-1]}\right)^{a}\cong\bigwedge^{i}H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})^{a}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})^{a}\cong\left(\frac{R^{i}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})}{(R^{i}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))[\zeta_{p}-1]}\right)^{a}.

(5.23)

If in addition,
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then the almost can be dropped.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.11
, each
H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})^{a}
has no nonzero 2-torsion, so the antisymmetry of the map (
5.22
), in each pair of the variables indeed induces the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+a}
-module map (
5.23
). For the isomorphism claim, we may work étale locally (on
\mathfrak{X}
), so we put ourselves in the situation of (
5.16
) (cf. Corollary
5.10
). Similar to the proof of
[
CK19
, Proposition 4.8]
, the edge maps (
4.5
) are compatible with cup products. By Theorem
4.6
and Remark
5.12
, it then remains to argue that via the cup product the identification

H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R)\cong R^{d}\text{ induces }H^{i}_{\cont}(\Delta,R)\cong\bigwedge^{i}(R^{d}).

This is precisely the content of
[
BMS18
, Lemmas 7.3 and 7.5]
.

The final statement follows from Lemma
5.1
and running the argument on the nose.
∎

To relate
H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
to Kähler differentials, we now review the needed material on cotangent complexes.

5.3.2
The completed cotangent complex
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}

We will need to analyze a cotangent complex naturally appearing on our (fibered product) site
D
. To phrase results, however, we need two facts coming from
[
BMS18
, §8.2]
(cf.
[
CK19
, §4.9]
):

(1)

\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathcal{O}}\cong\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/\mathcal{O}}\cong 0
and

(2)

\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{+}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}\{1\}[1]
.

We take some time to review them. Affinoid perfectoids form a basis of
X_{\proet}
by
[
Sch13
, Proposition 4.8]
. Therefore
[
BMS18
, Lemma 3.14]
ensures that for the sheaf of rings
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{+}
, the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathcal{O}}
, whose terms are
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{+}
-flat and which give an object of
D^{\leq 0}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{+})
, satisfies

\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\cong 0\text{ and therefore (by derived Nakayama) }\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathcal{O}}\cong 0.

This shows (1). We now show (2). By
[
BMS18
, Definition 8.2]
, the completed cotangent complex
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is the (shifted) Breuil-Kisin-Fargues twist
\mathcal{O}\{1\}[1]
and so the derived
p
-adic completion turns the canonical morphism

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}\to\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\text{ into an isomorphism }\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}\{1\}[1]\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}},

since by (1), the cone (of the said morphism)
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}/\mathcal{O}}\cong 0
.

Armed with (1)-(2) we turn to our analysis of
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}
and compare it to the sheaf
q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Proposition
5.18
). We will need some preparation:

Lemma 5.17
.

For a good object

Z=(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))
,

q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}(Z)=\Omega^{1}_{R/S}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}.

Proof.

A priori
q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is the sheaf associated to the presheaf
Z\mapsto\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{R/S}\otimes_{R}(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})
. On the other hand by
[
FK18
, Chapter I, Proposition 5.3.17]
,
\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{R/S}
is finite free
R
-module. Since the
Z
-sections
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}
are
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
(cf. Proposition
5.7
), it follows that the presheaf is already a sheaf.
∎

Proposition 5.18
.

The complex
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}
is quasi-isomorphic to
q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
placed in degree
0
.

Proof.

We use
[
Sta18
, Tag 0D6K]
and begin analyzing
\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
. By Lemma
4.1
(resp. Corollary
4.2
) together with the fact that good objects form a basis for the topology on
D
(cf. Proposition
2.16
),
p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}
(resp.
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}
) is
p
-torsion free on local sections. Therefore there is a canonical quasi-isomorphism (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 08QQ]
)

\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{L}_{(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})/(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}/p^{n})}.

(5.24)

Now for a good object

Z=(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))
,
H^{i}(\mathbb{L}_{(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})/(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}/p^{n})})
is the sheaf associated to the presheaf (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 08SW]
)

Z\mapsto H^{i}(\mathbb{L}_{(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty}/p^{n})/(S_{\infty}/p^{n})}).

(5.25)

However as
R/p^{n}
is smooth over
S/p^{n}
we obtain (again by
[
Sta18
, Tag 08QQ]
)

\mathbb{L}_{(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty}/p^{n})/(S_{\infty}/p^{n})}\cong\mathbb{L}_{(R/p^{n})/(S/p^{n})}\otimes_{S/p^{n}}S_{\infty}/p^{n}

is concentrated in degree 0. Therefore (
5.24
) implies
\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
is concentrated in degree 0. Hence by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0D6K]
,
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}
is also concentrated in degree
0
.

At degree 0,
H^{0}(\mathbb{L}_{(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty}/p^{n})/(S_{\infty}/p^{n})})
coincides with
\Omega^{1}_{(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty}/p^{n})/(S_{\infty}/p^{n})}
(cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 08UR]
). Thus by the description of, in particular
H^{0}(\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
(cf. (
5.25
)),

\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}(U,\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})=\varprojlim_{n}\Omega^{1}_{(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})/(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}/p^{n})}(U).

(5.26)

We now analyse the RHS of (
5.26
). The sheaf
\varprojlim_{n}\Omega^{1}_{(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})/(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}/p^{n})}
is associated to the presheaf
Z\mapsto\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/S_{\infty}}
. By
[
FK18
