, Chapter I, Proposition 5.1.5]
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\Omega^{1}_{R/S}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/S_{\infty}}.

(5.27)

Finally by lemma
5.17
,
\Omega^{1}_{R/S}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
are precisely the
Z
-sections of
q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{Y}/\mathfrak{X}}
.
∎

5.3.3
The relation between
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
and the sheaf of relative Kähler differentials

The functoriality of the cotangent complex supplies the pullback morphism

\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to R\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{+}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overset{(2)}{\cong}R\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}\{1\}[1].

(5.28)

We first relate
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
to the completed cotangent complex of the previous section:
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}^{+}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{+}}
. Indeed
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is the (shifted) Breuil-Kisin-Fargues twist
\mathcal{O}\{1\}[1]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Definition 8.2]
) and so

(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+})^{\wedge}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}\{1\}[1].

This implies
H^{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\cong H^{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}})\cong q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
, where the last isomorphism is Proposition
5.18
. Therefore by applying
H^{0}(-)
to (
5.28
) and twisting by
\mathcal{O}\{-1\}
we obtain:

q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-1\}\to R^{1}\nu_{f*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}\twoheadrightarrow\frac{R^{1}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X})}{(R^{1}\nu_{f*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X}))[\zeta_{p}-1]}\cong H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}).

(5.29)

Theorem 5.19
.

The composition (
5.29
) induces an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+a}
-isomorphism (after dividing the map by
\zeta_{p}-1
)

q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-1\}^{a}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})^{a},

(5.30)

which by Proposition
5.11
(in particular
H^{0}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
) and Proposition
5.16
, induces an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+a}
-module identification

q^{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-i\}^{a}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})^{a}\text{, for every }i\geq 0.

(5.31)

If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then the almost can be dropped.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.10
and Lemma
5.17
we may assume
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
and
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(S)
with
R=S\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{d}^{\pm 1}\}
. In this case
\Delta=\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)^{d}
is the natural group acting
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
-linearly on
R_{\infty}
. The
p
-adic derived completion of the pullback morphism

\mathbb{L}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}}R_{\infty}\to\mathbb{L}_{R_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

supplies a morphism
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{R_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cong R_{\infty}\{1\}[1]
. We remind the reader that the isomorphism
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{R_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cong R_{\infty}\{1\}[1]
is coming from the Breuil-Kisin-Fargues twist
\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}=\mathcal{O}\{1\}[1]
. The
\Delta
-equivariance of the map
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\to R_{\infty}
induces a map

H^{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\to H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})\{1\}

(5.32)

The LHS of (
5.32
)
H^{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{L}}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}})=\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}/S_{\infty}}\overset{\eqref{eq:FKlocsitu}}{\cong}\Omega^{1}_{R/S}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
is a free
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
-module of rank
d
generated by
\tfrac{dt_{i}}{t_{i}}
. By the proof of
[
BMS18
, Proposition 8.15]
(
5.32
) is an isomorphism onto

(\zeta_{p}-1)H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\{1\}=(\zeta_{p}-1)H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})\{1\}

(5.33)

Note the equality of (
5.33
) follows from Proposition
4.5
. The isomorphism sends
\tfrac{dt_{i}}{t_{i}}
to

d\log_{i}\otimes 1\in H^{1}_{\cont}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1),\mathcal{O}\{1\})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})=\Hom_{\cont}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1),T_{p}(\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}),

(5.34)

is the map on
p
-adic Tate modules induced by the map
d\log_{i}\colon\mu_{p^{\infty}}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{O}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. The
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
in (
5.34
) corresponds to the
i
th component in
\Delta
. The result now follows from Theorem
4.6
.
∎

5.4
The de Rham specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

In this section, we relate the de Rham specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
to the relative de Rham complex, cf.
[
BMS18
, Theorem 14.1]
and
[
CK19
, Theorem 4.17]
. The additional difficulty present in the relative situation is that in general, we only have almost isomorphisms (cf. (
5.4
)). This means we have to take care with the construction of the map (
5.35
). For the next theorem we abuse notation and define the complex
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
to have
i
th term
q^{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
and induced differentials.

Theorem 5.20
.

There is a morphism in
D(D,\mathcal{O})

q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O}.

(5.35)

whose image in
D(D,\mathcal{O}^{a})
is an almost isomorphism. If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then (
5.35
) is already an isomorphism in
D(D,\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

We have

A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O}\cong A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\varphi}A_{\Inf}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\cong L\eta_{\tilde{\xi}}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}

where the second isomorphism follows from
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.11]
and the identity
\varphi(\mu)=\tilde{\xi}\mu
. By
[
BMS18
, Proposition 6.12]
the object
L\eta_{\tilde{\xi}}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}
is identified with the complex
C^{\bullet}
whose
i
th degree term is

H^{i}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\tfrac{\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1})}{(\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}))^{2}}\right)^{\otimes i}

and the differentials are given by Bockstein homomorphisms. By the proof of
[
CK19
, Theorem 4.17]
,
\tfrac{\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1})}{(\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}))^{2}}\cong\mathcal{O}\{1\}
. The complex
C^{\bullet}
has a natural structure of a commutative differential graded
\mathcal{O}
-algebra with multiplication induced by the composition:

H^{i}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}/\tilde{\xi})\times H^{j}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}/\tilde{\xi})\to H^{i+j}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}/\tilde{\xi}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}}A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}/\tilde{\xi})\to H^{i+j}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}/\tilde{\xi}),

where the first map is the cup product and the second map is induced from the lax symmetric monoidal structure on
L\eta_{\mu}
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 6.7]
) and the commutative algebra structure on
R\nu_{f*}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}
.

The strategy now for producing a morphism
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to C^{\bullet}
in
D(D,\mathcal{O})
is as follows:

(1)

Construct a morphism
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}\to C^{0}
.

(2)

Construct a (compatible) morphism
q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to C^{1}
(universal property of Kähler differentials).

(3)

Extend the morphism from (2) to higher forms using the differential graded
\mathcal{O}
-algebra structure of
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
and
C^{\bullet}
.

A problem arises in step (2), as we do not know in general whether
H^{1}(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O})
is (derived)
p
-adically complete. In the case
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
however, this is indeed true
12
12

12

This follows from Theorem
5.2
, Proposition
5.11
and Lemma
5.8
.
. Moreover with regards to step (3), we do not know whether
C^{2}
is 2-torsion free (in the case
p=2
), in order to pass to exterior algebras. A solution for these obstacles is to work with the (intermediary) group cohomology terms appearing as the source of (
5.8
). More precisely for each good object
Z
of the form (
5.1
) we have a map of complexes

\textstyle{C^{0}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{C^{1}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{2}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{C^{0}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{1}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{2}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

(5.36)

where
C^{i}_{Z}:=H^{i}(L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O})\{i\}
. The top complex is a representative for the complex
L\eta_{\tilde{\xi}}L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}
via
[
BMS18
, Proposition 6.12]
. The vertical arrows are induced via (
5.8
) and the functoriality of the Bockstein homomorphism provides the map (
5.36
).

We now construct a morphism of complexes
(q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)\to C^{\bullet}_{Z}
(the complex
(q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)
has
i
th term
(q^{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)
) using the strategy outlined above.

(1)

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)=R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\overset{\eqref{eq:lonconcesd}}{=}\frac{H^{0}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})}{H^{0}_{\cont}(\Delta,R_{\infty})[\zeta_{p}-1]}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:truisnoalis}^{-1}}C^{0}_{Z}
.

(2)

Note that the complex
C^{i}_{Z}
is a commutative differential graded
\mathcal{O}
-algebra (by a similar argument used to show
C^{\bullet}
is a commutative differential graded
\mathcal{O}
-algebra). By (
5.7
) and (
5.17
),
C^{1}_{Z}
is
p
-adically complete. Therefore to construct a compatible morphism
(q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)\to C^{1}_{Z}
, we need to check that the composition
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)\to C^{0}_{Z}\to C^{1}_{Z}
is
S_{\infty}
-linear (we endow
C^{0}_{Z}
and
C^{1}_{Z}
with their natural structure of
S_{\infty}
-modules via the identifications (
5.7
) and (
5.17
)). By definition the first map
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)\to C^{0}_{Z}
is
S_{\infty}
-linear. To see that the Bockstein homomorphism
C^{0}_{Z}\to C^{1}_{Z}
is
S_{\infty}
-linear, note that
L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))
has an explicit representation by a complex of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
-modules involving Koszul complexes (cf.
[
Bha18
, Lemma 4.6]
13
13

13

Technically the proof in loc.cit. is for the ring
A_{\Inf}
, however the same proof works for
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
(and its étale extensions).
and
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.9]
). This means that the Bockstein homomorphism is also
S_{\infty}
-linear. Note that the
S_{\infty}
-module structures put on
C^{0}_{Z}
and
C^{1}_{Z}
are compatible with the ones coming from the Koszul complexes.

(3)

By (
5.7
), (
5.17
) and Corollary
4.2
,
C^{2}_{Z}
is 2-torsion free. Thus the map constructed in (2) extends (compatibly) to higher forms.

We now show that the morphisms
(q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)\to C^{\bullet}_{Z}\to C^{\bullet}(Z)
are functorial in
Z
, and therefore glue together to give a morphism of complexes
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to C^{\bullet}
. The functoriality of the first map follows from the universal property of Kähler differentials. The functoriality of the second map follows from the functoriality of (
5.8
), which in turn follows from the functoriality of the edge map (
4.15
).

It remains to show that the map of complexes
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to C^{\bullet}
is an almost isomorphism (and an isomorphism in the case
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
). By Theorem
5.2
there is a morphism

C^{1}\to H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\{1\}

which is an almost isomorphism. We claim that the induced composition

q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to C^{1}\to H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\{1\}

(5.37)

identifies with (
5.30
){1}. We reduce to the case
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
and
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(S)
with
R=S\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{d}^{\pm 1}\}
(cf. Lemma
5.9
). We consider the morphisms upon taking
Z
-sections with
Z
of the form (
5.1
). In this case, as shown in the proof of Theorem
5.19
, (
5.30
){1} looks like (using the notation appearing there):

\displaystyle(q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)

\displaystyle\to H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\{1\}\xrightarrow{H^{1}(\text{\eqref{eq:fsmsfdssd}})\{1\}}H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\{1\}(Z)

\displaystyle\tfrac{dt_{i}}{t_{i}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\tfrac{d\log_{i}\otimes 1}{\zeta_{p}-1}.

On the other hand, (
5.37
) fits into a diagram

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{C^{0}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{1}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\{1\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H^{1}(\text{\eqref{eq:fsmsfdssd}})\{1\}}
\textstyle{C^{1}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\{1\}(Z),}

where
C^{1}_{Z}\to H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})\{1\}
is an isomorphism induced by
(
5.7
). So we need to understand the morphism
(q^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)\to C^{1}_{Z}
(constructed by universality of Kähler differentials). For this we can assume
d=1
. By
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.9]
, the complex
L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))
is represented by explicit Koszul complexes:

L\eta_{\mu}R\Gamma_{\cont}(\Delta,\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty}))\cong\widehat{\bigoplus}_{a\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})\xrightarrow{\tfrac{[\epsilon^{pa}]-1}{[\epsilon]-1}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}))=:\widehat{\bigoplus}_{a\in\mathbb{Z}}K_{a}

where the completion is
(p,\mu)
-adic (cf.
[
Bha18
, Lemma 4.6]
). On grading of degree
a
, the Bockstein homomorphism (after trivializing the twist
\{1\}
by choosing
\tilde{\xi}
as a generator of
(\tilde{\xi})
) looks like

\displaystyle H^{0}(K_{a}/\tilde{\xi})

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(K_{a}/\tilde{\xi})

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(\tfrac{[\epsilon^{pa}]-1}{[\epsilon]-1}\right)/\tilde{\xi}=\left(\tfrac{[\epsilon^{pa}]-1}{[\epsilon]-1}\right)/\left(\tfrac{[\epsilon^{p}]-1}{[\epsilon]-1}\right)=\tfrac{[\epsilon^{pa}]-1}{[\epsilon^{p}]-1}=a\mod\tilde{\xi}.

Moreover
t_{1}^{a}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)
is identified with
1\in H^{0}(K_{a}/\tilde{\xi})
and
t_{1}^{a}\in H^{1}_{\cont}(\Delta,R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})
identifies with
1\in H^{1}(K_{a}/\tilde{\xi})
. The
disappearance
of
\tfrac{d\log_{1}}{\zeta_{p}-1}
is explained by the fact that the image of
\widetilde{\xi}
via the map
\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1})\to\tfrac{\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1})}{(\ker(\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}))^{2}}\cong\mathcal{O}\{1\}
is
\tfrac{d\log_{1}}{\zeta_{p}-1}
(cf.
[
Bha18
, Remark 6.9]
and
[
BMS18
, Example 4.24]
). For an explicit calculation, cf.
[
Bha12
, Remark 9.4]
. The result now follows.
∎

An important immediate consequence of the de Rham specialization is the following (almost is w.r.t.
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
.):

Corollary 5.21
.

The object
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is almost derived
p
-adically complete (i.e. the canonical morphism
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to R\varprojlim(A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
is an almost isomorphism). Similarly for the object
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
.

Proof.

First note that by Lemma
5.8
,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
is derived
p
-adically complete. This implies
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is a bounded complex each of whose terms is derived
p
-adically complete and hence itself is derived
p
-adically complete (induction via stupid truncations).
Thus by Theorem
5.20
,
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O}
is almost derived
p
-adically complete. By induction on
n
, one gets that
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/\xi^{n}
is almost derived
p
-adically complete. Moreover by Corollary
5.13
,
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is almost derived
\xi
-adically complete. Therefore
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is almost derived
p
-adically complete by the definition of derived completeness (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0999]
). The last statement follows by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
.
∎

Remark 5.22
.

As in Remark
5.14
, in general the “almost” in Corollary
5.21
cannot be dropped even in the case
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
.

5.5
Base change for
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}

In this section, in addition to the assumptions made in the beginning of §
5
, we assume that
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is proper. For later use, in particular for the comparison of
A_{\Inf}
-cohomology with
q
-crystalline cohomology (cf. Theorem
6.33
) we record a base change result, cf. Proposition
5.24
. For a similar statement involving the Faltings’ topos, cf.
[
AG22
, Théorème 6.5.31]
. Recall by (
2.4
), the projections
p
and
q
extend to morphisms of ringed topoi
(p,p^{\sharp})
and
(q,q^{\sharp})
. By taking modulo
p^{n}
(of the structure sheaves), we denote the induced morphisms by
(p_{n},p_{n}^{\sharp})
and
(q_{n},q_{n}^{\sharp})
, respectively. Similarly we obtain
f_{n}
and
\nu_{Y,n}
. First we need some preparation.

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
g\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
be a morphism of affine
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b) over
\mathcal{O}
. For each
n\geq 1
, consider the commutative diagram of ringed topoi

\textstyle{(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(q_{n},q_{n}^{\sharp})}}
\scriptstyle{(p_{n},p_{n}^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(g_{n},g_{n}^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{(Y_{\proet},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(\nu_{Y,n},\nu_{Y,n}^{\sharp})}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{Y}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}).}

Then the cohomology sheaves of the cone of the canonical base change map

\nu_{Y,n}^{*}g_{n*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})\to p_{n*}q_{n}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})

(5.38)

are killed by
\mathfrak{m}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
,
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(S)
and
Z:=\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})
an affinoid perfectoid of
Y_{\proet}
.
By
[
Sch13
, Lemma 4.10(i)]
the LHS of (
5.38
) is almost equal the sheafification of the presheaf

Z\mapsto\varinjlim_{\mathfrak{V}\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\et},Z\to V}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})(\mathfrak{X}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{V})\otimes_{(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n})(\mathfrak{V})}S_{\infty}/p^{n}=(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty})/p^{n},

where
V
is the adic generic fiber of
\mathfrak{V}
and
Z\to V
are morphisms over
Y
. Moreover by Lemma
5.5
(i) and Proposition
5.7
, the RHS of (
5.38
) is almost equal to the sheafification of the presheaf

Z\mapsto(R\otimes_{S}S_{\infty})/p^{n}

∎

Proposition 5.24
.

For each
n\geq 1
, consider the commutative diagram of ringed topoi

\textstyle{(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(q_{n},q_{n}^{\sharp})}}
\scriptstyle{(p_{n},p_{n}^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f_{n},f_{n}^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{(Y_{\proet},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(\nu_{Y,n},\nu_{Y,n}^{\sharp})}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{Y}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}),}

and
\mathscr{F}
a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}
-module. Then the cohomology sheaves of the cone of the canonical base change map (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 07A7]
)

L\nu_{Y,n}^{*}Rf_{n*}\mathscr{F}\to Rp_{n*}Lq_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}

(5.39)

are killed by
\mathfrak{m}
.

Proof.

The statement is local for the étale topology on
\mathfrak{Y}
, so we can assume
\mathfrak{Y}
is affine. The idea is to rewrite both sides of (
5.39
) as sheafified Čech complexes and then compare these Čech complexes via Leray’s acyclicity lemma.

We deal with the LHS of (
5.39
) first.
Since
f_{n}
is proper, we can choose a finite affine open covering
\mathcal{U}:\mathfrak{X}=\bigcup\mathfrak{U}_{i}
, which trivializes
\mathscr{F}
. Then we are in the set-up of
[
Sta18
, Tag 01XL]
. In particular since
f_{n}
is flat we have an explicit
K
-flat resolution of
Rf_{n*}\mathscr{F}
:
\check{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{U},f_{n},\mathscr{F})
, whose
r
th term is

\check{\mathcal{C}}^{r}(\mathcal{U},f_{n},\mathscr{F})=\bigoplus_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}f_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})\lvert_{\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}},

where
\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}:=\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}}\cap\ldots\cap\mathfrak{U}_{i_{r}}
and
f_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is the restriction of
f_{n}
to
\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}
. Therefore the LHS of (
5.39
) is represented by a complex whose
r
th term is

\bigoplus_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}\nu_{Y,n}^{*}f_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})\lvert_{\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}}.

(5.40)

By Lemma
5.23
the expression (
5.40
) is almost equal to

\bigoplus_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}p_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}},

(5.41)

where
p_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}\colon Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}\to Y_{\proet}
is the obvious map (considered as a morphism of ringed topoi with the obvious structure sheaves modulo
p^{n}
).

We now deal with the RHS of (
5.39
), which is

Rp_{n*}(q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})
. Let
Z_{i}=(Y\to\mathfrak{Y}\leftarrow\mathfrak{U}_{i})
. The covering
\mathcal{U}
gives rise to a covering
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}:\{Z_{i}\to(Y\to\mathfrak{Y}\leftarrow\mathfrak{X})\}_{i}
. We claim that
q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}
has an (almost) resolution by a complex
\check{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})
, whose
r
th term is

\check{\mathcal{C}}^{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})=\bigoplus_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}g_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n}\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}}

where
g_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}\colon Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}\to Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
(considered as a morphism of ringed topoi with the obvious structure sheaves modulo
p^{n}
). The differentials are the usual ones attached to a Čech complex and
\varepsilon\colon q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to\check{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})
is defined by taking the direct product of the natural maps
q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to g_{n,i*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n}\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i,\et}}
. The claim may appear standard but without a study of points for our fibered topos
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
(cf. the proof of
[
Sta18
, Tag 02FU]
), we rely on the almost vanishing of higher Čech cohomology, cf. proof of Lemma
5.5
(i).

Lemma 5.25
.

The sequence of sheaves

0\to q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}^{a}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\check{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})^{a}\to\check{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})^{a}\to\ldots

is exact.

Proof.

Let
Z
be a good object of
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
, which trivializes
q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}
. Then it suffices to show that

0\to(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+a}_{D}/p^{n})(Z)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\check{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})^{a}(Z)\to\check{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n})^{a}(Z)\to\ldots

is exact. Together with Proposition
5.7
, this is proved in the proof of Lemma
5.5
(i).
∎

Returning to the proof of the proposition, again by almost vanishing of higher Čech cohomology, we have

R^{s}g_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n}\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}}\text{ and }R^{s}p_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n}\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}}

are almost zero for
s>0
. This implies
R^{s}p_{n*}(g_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{D}^{+}/p^{n}\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{U}_{i_{0}\ldots i_{r},\et}})
is almost zero for
s>0
(because
p_{n}\circ g_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}=p_{n,i_{0}\ldots i_{r}}
). Therefore by Leray’s acyclicity lemma we have

p_{n*}\check{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})^{a}=Rp_{n*}\check{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})^{a}=Rp_{n*}q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}^{a}.

Finally note that
p_{n*}\check{\mathcal{C}}^{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}},q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F})
gives the expression in (
5.41
). This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.26
.

In the situation of Proposition
5.24
, assume in addition that for all
i\geq 0
,
R^{i}f_{n*}\mathscr{F}
is locally free of finite type. Then canonical base change map (
5.39
), induces morphisms

\nu_{Y,n}^{*}R^{i}f_{n*}\mathscr{F}\to R^{i}p_{n*}q_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}

whose kernel and cokernel are killed by
\mathfrak{m}
.

Proof.

This is obtained by taking the cohomology of (
5.39
) in Proposition
5.24
.
∎

We also record a
p
-completed version of the base change.

Corollary 5.27
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}
-module. Then the cohomology sheaves of the cone of the canonical base change map

L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\mathscr{F}\to Rp_{*}Lq^{*}\mathscr{F}

are killed by
\mathfrak{m}
.

Proof.

First note that by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A1H]
,
Rf_{*}\mathscr{F}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}
is a perfect object of
D(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n})
and its formation commutes with base change. Therefore by taking the derived limit we obtain
Rf_{*}\mathscr{F}
itself is a perfect object of
D(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}})
. Since
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}
is
p
-adically derived complete by
[
Zav21
, Lemma C.11]
, we obtain
L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\mathscr{F}
is
p
-adically derived complete. Moreover by Lemma
5.8
and
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A0G]
,
Rp_{*}Lq^{*}\mathscr{F}
is also derived
p
-adically complete. Therefore the result follows by taking the derived limit along
n\geq 1
in Proposition
5.24
(note that the derived limit of almost zero objects is again almost zero).
∎

Finally we establish a base change result for our de Rham complex
q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
. We remind the reader that the complex is defined as a complex whose
i
th term is
q^{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
with induced differentials. In particular the differentials are non-linear so this complex lives in
D(D,p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y})
and not in
D(D,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D})
(even though each term is a
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
-module). Therefore the following result serves as an appropriate substitute for Proposition
5.24
.

Corollary 5.28
.

For each
n\geq 1
, consider the commutative diagram of ringed topoi

\textstyle{(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(\tilde{q}_{n},\tilde{q}_{n}^{\sharp})}}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{p}_{n},\tilde{p}_{n}^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{X}_{\et},f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{f}_{n},\tilde{f}_{n}^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{(Y_{\proet},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/p^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(\nu_{Y,n},\nu_{Y,n}^{\sharp})}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{Y}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}),}

and
\mathscr{F}
a bounded complex each of whose terms are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}
-modules (with differentials
f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}
-linear). Then the cohomology sheaves of the cone of the canonical base change map

L\nu_{Y,n}^{*}R\tilde{f}_{n*}\mathscr{F}\to R\tilde{p}_{n*}L\tilde{q}_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}

(5.42)

are killed by
\mathfrak{m}
. By taking derived limits (as
n
goes to
\infty
) a version of the statement also holds for a bounded complex each of whose terms are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}
-modules (cf. Corollary
5.27
).

Proof.

By using stupid truncations, we immediately reduce to the case where
\mathscr{F}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}
-module (viewed as an
f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}
-module). In this case the LHS of (
5.42
) coincides with the LHS of (
5.39
). We now compare
L\tilde{q}_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}
and
Lq_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}
as sheaves. The key point is that the tensor product defining
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}
(cf. Definition
2.5
) is derived because
f
is flat.

\displaystyle Lq_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle=q_{n}^{-1}\mathscr{F}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/p^{n}

\displaystyle=q_{n}^{-1}\mathscr{F}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}}q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}\otimes_{p^{-1}\nu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}}^{\mathbb{L}}p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/p^{n}

\displaystyle=q_{n}^{-1}\mathscr{F}\otimes_{p^{-1}\nu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}/p^{n}}^{\mathbb{L}}p^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y}/p^{n}

\displaystyle=L\tilde{q}_{n}^{*}\mathscr{F}.

Therefore the RHS of (
5.42
) coincides with the RHS of (
5.39
). The result now follows by Proposition
5.24
.
∎

6
q
-crystalline site

The main goal of this section is to compare the objects
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
and
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
considered thus far with the
q
-crystalline site of Bhatt-Scholze.
Let
q
be a formal parameter. We introduce a global (big) version of the
q
-crystalline site of
[
BS22
, §16]
. We advise the reader to familiarise themselves with the contents of §16 in loc.cit. before continuing. The logarithmic context is studied in
[
Kos21
, §7]
.

As usual we denote by
A=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[q-1]]
(the
(p,q-1)
-adic completion of
\mathbb{Z}[q]
) with
\delta
-structure given by
\delta(q)=0
. Let
[p]_{q}:=\tfrac{q^{p}-1}{q-1}
be the
q
-analogue of
p
. This gives the
q
-crystalline prism
(A,([p]_{q}))
. The
q
-analogue of divided power structures is handled by the following: if
x
is an element of a
[p]_{q}
-torsion free
\delta
-
A
-algebra
D
such that
\phi(x)\in[p]_{q}D
, then we write

\gamma(x):=\tfrac{\phi(x)}{[p]_{q}}-\delta(x).

In order to define a big version of the
q
-crystalline site, we slightly modify the notion of a
q
-PD pair in
[
BS22
]
(cf.
[
Kos21
, §7.1]
).

Definition 6.1

(
q
-PD pair)
.

A
q
-PD pair is given by a derived
(p,[p]_{q})
-complete
\delta
-pair
(D,I)
over
(A,(q-1))
such that

(1)

(D,([p]_{q}))
is a bounded prism over
(A,([p]_{q}))
(in particular
D
is
[p]_{q}
-torsion free),

(2)

\phi(I)\subset[p]_{q}D
and
\gamma(I)\subset I
,

(3)

D/(q-1)
is
p
-torsion free with finite
(p,[p]_{q})
-complete Tor amplitude over
D
and

(4)

D/I
is
p
-adically complete.

Remark 6.2
.

As observed in
[
Kos21
]
, the addition of condition (4) to
[
BS22
, Definition 16.2]
will be convenient to define the big
q
-crystalline site (without the use of some derived geometry).

The following lemma is a special case of
[
Kos21
, Lemma 7.3]
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
(D,I)
be a
q
-PD pair. If
D/I\to\overline{E}
is
p
-completely étale, there exists a unique
q
-PD pair
(E,J)
over
(D,I)
lifting
D/I\to\overline{E}
.

Proof.

First note that there is a unique
(p,[p]_{q})
-completely étale lift
D\to E
of
D/I\to\overline{E}
(because for any
i\in I
,
i^{p}\in(p,[p]_{q})
). The kernel
J
of the map
E\to\overline{E}
is given by the
(p,[p]_{q})
-adic completion of
IE
. We equip
E
with the unique
\delta
-structure compatible with the one on
D
(cf.
[
BS22
, Lemma 2.18]
). It remains to check conditions (1)-(4) of Definition
6.1
for the
\delta
-pair
(E,J)
. Since
E
is
(p,[p]_{q})
-completely flat over
D
, it follows that conditions (1) and (3) are satisfied. Moreover by construction
E/J=\overline{E}
is
p
-adically complete and so condition (4) is satisfied. It remains to check the first half of condition (2) (the second half is shown in the proof of
[
Kos21
, Lemma 7.3]
). Since
D\to E
is a map of
\delta
-rings over
A
, it follows that
\phi(IE)\subset[p]_{q}E
. This implies that
\phi(J)\subset[p]_{q}E
because
[p]_{q}E
is closed for the
(p,[p]_{q})
-adic topology on
E
.
∎

Fix a
q
-PD pair
(D,I)
and let
\mathfrak{Z}
be a
p
-adic formal scheme over
D/I
. We can now define the big
q
-crystalline site
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Z}/D)
(cf.
[
Kos21
, Definition 7.5]
) and its associated topos.

Definition 6.4

(The
q
-crystalline site and topos)
.

We define the (big)
q
-crystalline site
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Z}/D)
as the opposite of the following category: an object is a
q
-PD pair
(E,J)
over
(D,I)
and a
\Spf(D/I)
-morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes
\Spf(E/J)\to\mathfrak{Z}
. Morphisms are the obvious ones. We endow it with the étale topology (by Lemma
6.3
there exists fiber products of
q
-PD pairs when one of the morphisms is étale in the obvious sense) with covers being singletons. The corresponding topos will be denoted by
(\mathfrak{Z}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. When
\mathfrak{Z}=\Spf(T)
is affine we will denote the site and topos by
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(T/D)
and
(T/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
, respectively.

The first version of Definition
6.4
(with the Zariski topology in place of the étale topology) appears in
[
BS22
, Remark 16.15(2)]
.

Definition 6.5

(structure sheaf)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
denote the structure presheaves
(E,J)\mapsto E
and
(E,J)\mapsto E/J
. These are sheaves by definition of coverings.

For
\mathfrak{Z}=\Spf(T)
affine, there is the big
q
-crystalline topos
(T/D)_{q-\textnormal{crys}}
with underlying site
q-\textnormal{crys}(T/D)
defined in
[
BS22
, Definition 16.12]
with structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{crys}}
. As a reality check, we show that in this case the cohomology does not depend on whether one uses the Zariski or étale topology.

Lemma 6.6
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{Z}=\Spf(T)
. Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

R\Gamma((T/D)_{q-\textnormal{crys}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{crys}})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma((T/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})

(6.1)

Proof.

The inclusion of sites
q-\textnormal{crys}(T/D)\to q-\textnormal{CRYS}(T/D)
is continuous and induces a morphism of topoi

g\colon(T/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to(T/D)_{q-\textnormal{crys}}

(6.2)

with
g^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{crys}}=\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. The morphism (
6.2
) induces the morphism (
6.1
). Choosing a weakly final object as in
[
BS22
, Construction 16.13]
in
q-\textnormal{crys}(T/D)
, one obtains a Čech-Alexander complex. This is an explicit representative for the RHS of (
6.1
). It is easy to check (by freeness) that this weakly final object is also weakly final in the big site
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(T/D)
. Therefore by Čech theory the same Čech-Alexander complex is an explicit representative for the LHS of (
6.1
). Finally the formation of the Čech-Alexander complex is compatible with the morphism (
6.1
).
∎

Recall the small étale site of
\mathfrak{Z}
is denoted by
\mathfrak{Z}_{\et}
. We denote the big étale site of
\mathfrak{Z}
by
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}
and
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
the site consisting of affine
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathfrak{Z}
whose covers are singleton étale maps. If there is no confusion we will use the same notation for their topoi
\mathfrak{Z}_{\et}
,
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}
and
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
, respectively.

As usual we have a cocontinuous and continuous functor (by Lemma
6.3
):

\displaystyle q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Z}/D)

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}

\displaystyle(E,J,\Spf(E/J)\to\mathfrak{Z})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\Spf(E/J)\to\mathfrak{Z}),

which sits in a diagram of functors of sites

q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Z}/D)\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}\leftarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{\et}.

(6.3)

The last two functors induce morphisms of sites and applying
[
Sta18
, Tag 00XO]
to the first functor we obtain a diagram of topoi

(\mathfrak{Z}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}\xleftarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\et},

(6.4)

which gives a map of topoi
u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Z}}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\et}
. In the next section we will construct an explicit underlying site for the oriented product of topoi

(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},

(6.5)

which will serve as the
q
-crystalline analogue for the fiber product
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
appearing in the pro-étale setting.

6.1
Product of topoi revisited

We now assume that we have a
\Spf(D/I)
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes. The oriented product (
6.5
) certainly exists by the universal property
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI, Théorème 1.4]
and the fact that any morphism of topoi essentially comes from a morphism of sites (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 03A2]
). The difficulty in the current situation is that the morphism
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
is induced by some composition (
6.4
) (in particular it does not come directly from a morphism of the sites) and so the same construction for an underlying site (for the oriented product) given in
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI]
does not work. However, it turns out that (
6.4
) has enough properties to recover an explicit site for (
6.5
).

Remark 6.7
.

Regarding the construction of an underlying site, the fact that the left edge morphism
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
does not come from a morphism of the sites forces us to work with an oriented product of topoi rather than a fiber product. Moreover the right edge morphism
\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
does come from a morphism of sites and so the choice of orientation (
6.5
) is a natural candidate over
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overrightarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
. This is in contrast to our work on the pro-étale side, where we had the freedom to work with the fiber product (in the case
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are of type (S)(b))
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
or either of the two oriented products.

For the time being we work in general with the set up being as follows:

C_{1}
,
C_{2}
,
E
,
E^{\prime}
and
D
are five sites such that

(1)

E
is subcanonical, and
C_{2}
and
D
admit finite projective limits,

(2)

there is a diagram of functors

C_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{a}E\xleftarrow{g}D

(6.6)

where
g_{1}
is both continuous and cocontinuous, and the last two functors induce morphisms of sites
E\to E^{\prime}
and
E\to D
,

(3)

E\to E^{\prime}
induces an equivalence of topoi, and

(4)

there is a functor
f_{2}\colon D\to C_{2}
commuting with finite projective limits, which induces a morphism of sites
C_{2}\to D
.

Denote
f_{1}\colon C_{1}\to E
the composition
a\circ g_{1}
. Let
C
be the category of 5-tuples

(U,V,W,f_{1}(U)\to g(W),V\to f_{2}(W))

where
U,V
and
W
are objects of
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
D
, respectively, and
f_{1}(U)\to g(W)
and
V\to f_{2}(W)
are morphisms in
E
and
C_{2}
, respectively. We will denote such an object by
(U\to W\leftarrow V)
. Let
(U\to W\leftarrow V)
and
(U^{\prime}\to W^{\prime}\leftarrow V^{\prime})
be two objects of
D
. A morphism from
(U\to W\leftarrow V)
into
(U^{\prime}\to W^{\prime}\leftarrow V^{\prime})
is the datum of three morphisms
U\to U^{\prime}
,
V\to V^{\prime}
and
W\to W^{\prime}
in
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
D
, respectively such that the diagrams

\textstyle{f_{1}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{1}(U^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g(W^{\prime}),}

\textstyle{f_{2}(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{2}(W^{\prime})}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

are commutative. We equip
C
with the topology generated by coverings

\left\{(U_{i}\to W_{i}\leftarrow V_{i})\to(U\to W\leftarrow V)\right\}_{i\in I}

of the following three types:

(1)

V_{i}=V,W_{i}=W\text{ }
for all
i
and
(U_{i}\to U)_{i\in I}
is a covering.

(2)

U_{i}=U,W_{i}=W\text{ }
for all
i
and
(V_{i}\to V)_{i\in I}
is a covering.

(3)

I
is a singleton:
(U\to W\leftarrow V)\to(U^{\prime}\to W^{\prime}\leftarrow V^{\prime})
, with
U=U^{\prime}
and
V\to V^{\prime}
is induced by base change from
W\to W^{\prime}
.

Denote the resulting topos by
\widetilde{C}
.

Note that condition (4) gives a morphism of topoi
\widetilde{C}_{2}\to\widetilde{D}
and treating
g_{1}
as a cocontinuous functor, conditions (2)-(3) naturally give a morphism of topoi
\widetilde{C}_{1}\to\widetilde{D}
(the morphism of topoi
E^{\prime}\to E
is given by the adjoint functors
a^{s}
and
a_{s}
). One can therefore consider the oriented product of topoi
\widetilde{C}_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\widetilde{D}}\widetilde{C}_{2}
. We now come to the main result of this section, which gives an explicit description of an underlying site for the said oriented product:

Proposition 6.8
.

There is a canonical equivalence of topoi
\widetilde{C}_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\widetilde{D}}\widetilde{C}_{2}\simeq\widetilde{C}
.

Proof.

We first rewrite the objects of
C
which will make the comparison with the construction given in
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI, 1.1]
easier. Since
E
is subcanconical, the datum of a morphism
f_{1}(U)\to g(W)
in
E
is equivalent to giving a morphism in the topos
\widetilde{E}
between the associated representable sheaves

h_{f_{1}(U)}\to h_{g(W)}.

(6.7)

By
[
Sta18
, Tag 04D3]
, (
6.7
) takes the shape
a_{s}g_{1,s}h_{U}^{a}\to g_{s}h_{W}^{a}
(where
h_{U}^{a}
and
h_{W}^{a}
are the associated sheaves of
h_{U}
and
h_{W}
, respectively). Now
a_{s}
is a left adjoint to
a^{s}
and
g_{1,s}
is left adjoint to
g_{1}^{s}
(this is because both
a
and
g_{1}
are continuous functors of sites). Thus (
6.7
) is equivalent to giving a morphism

h_{U}^{a}\to g_{1}^{s}a^{s}g_{s}h_{W}^{a}.

(6.8)

Finally note that
g_{1}^{s}a^{s}g_{s}
is precisely the pullback of the morphism of topoi
\widetilde{C}_{1}\to\widetilde{D}
.

Having rewritten the objects of
C
, it remains to compare our construction of
C
with the construction in
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI, 1.1]
. By
[
Sta18
, Tag 03A2]
we can
enlarge
the site
C_{1}
to a site
C^{\prime}
such that the morphism of topoi
\widetilde{C}_{1}\to\widetilde{D}
is equivalent to a morphism of topoi
\widetilde{C^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{D}
induced from a morphism of sites
C^{\prime}\to D
. In fact we choose
C^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{C}_{1}
as in the proof of loc.cit. together with the inclusion of the final sheaf in the first inductive step (in particular
C^{\prime}
admits finite projective limits). Moreover
C^{\prime}
contains the objects
h_{U}^{a}
. Let
u\colon D\to C^{\prime}
be the corresponding functor (it is given by
W\mapsto g_{1}^{s}a^{s}g_{s}h_{W}^{a}
by the proof of
[
Sta18
, Tag 03A2]
). Note that
u
commutes with finite projective limits. Denote by
\underline{C}
the underlying site of
\widetilde{C}_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\widetilde{D}}\widetilde{C}_{2}
associated to the morphism of sites
C^{\prime}\to D
and
C_{2}\to D
, as given by
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI, 1.1]
. There is a functor of sites

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\to\underline{C}

\displaystyle(U,V,W,f_{1}(U)\to g(W),V\to f_{2}(W))

\displaystyle\mapsto(h^{a}_{U},V,W,h^{a}_{U}\to u(W),V\to f_{2}(W)),

which is special cocontinuous (because
C_{1}\to C^{\prime}
is special cocontinuous). The associated morphism of topoi induced by the cocontinuity of
C\to\underline{C}
,
\widetilde{C}\to\widetilde{\underline{C}}
is then an equivalence by
[
Sta18
, Tag 03A0]
.
∎

We will denote the site
C
by
C_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{D}C_{2}
. As usual there are natural projections

p_{1}\colon\widetilde{C}_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\widetilde{D}}\widetilde{C}_{2}\rightarrow\widetilde{C}_{1}\text{ and }p_{2}\colon\widetilde{C}_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\widetilde{D}}\widetilde{C}_{2}\rightarrow\widetilde{C}_{2}.

Since
C_{1}
may lack a final object, the description of
p_{2}
given in
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI, §1.3]
does not work. Nevertheless one can describe it as follows:

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
e_{C_{2}}
and
e_{D}
be the final objects of
C_{2}
and
D
. Then
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are induced by the functors of sites

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle\to C_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{D}C_{2}

\displaystyle C_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{D}C_{2}

\displaystyle\to C_{2}

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle\mapsto(U\to e_{D}\leftarrow e_{C_{2}})\text{ \hskip 39.83385pt and}

\displaystyle(U\to W\leftarrow V)

\displaystyle\mapsto V,

respectively. The first functor induces a morphism of sites and the second functor is cocontinuous.

Proof.

We use the notation in the proof of Proposition
6.8
. Let
e_{C^{\prime}}
be the final object of
C^{\prime}
. Then the projections
\widetilde{\underline{C}}\to\widetilde{C}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\underline{C}}\to\widetilde{C}_{2}
are induced by the functors of sites

\displaystyle C^{\prime}

\displaystyle\to\underline{C}

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle\to\underline{C}

\displaystyle U^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto(U^{\prime}\to e_{D}\leftarrow e_{C_{2}})\text{ \hskip 39.83385pt and}

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto(e_{C^{\prime}}\to e_{D}\leftarrow V),

respectively (cf.
[
ILO14
, Exposé XI, §1.3]
). The second functor (and also the first) has a cocontinuous left adjoint given by

\displaystyle\underline{C}

\displaystyle\to C_{2}

\displaystyle(U^{\prime}\to W\leftarrow V)

\displaystyle\mapsto V

which by
[
Sta18
, Tag 00XY]
induces the same morphism of topoi as its continuous right adjoint. The claims for
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
now follow from the commutativity of the diagram of sites

\textstyle{\underline{C}}
\textstyle{C^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{D}C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\underline{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}\overleftarrow{\times}_{D}C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{2},}

respectively.
∎

Let us now return to the situation of interest (compare (
6.3
) with (
6.6
)): the five sites under consideration are
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)
,
\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
,
\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}
,
\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
and
\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
, respectively. We have verified in the previous section that these five sites satisfy the conditions (1)-(4). Thus by Proposition
6.8
we have constructed an explicit underlying site for the oriented product (
6.5
). By Lemma
6.9
we have a simple description for the natural projections

p_{1}\colon(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\text{ and }p_{2}\colon(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{\et},

and there is a 2-map
fp_{2}\to u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}}p_{1}
.

Definition 6.10
.

We define the structure sheaf on
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
by the
p
-adically completed tensor product

\overline{\mathcal{O}}:=p_{1}^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\widehat{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}.

Remark 6.11
.

A word of explanation for the definition of
\overline{\mathcal{O}}
. The morphism
p_{1}^{-1}(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}\to p_{1}^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
is induced by the map
(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}\to\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. The morphism
p_{1}^{-1}(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}\to p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}
is induced by the composition

p_{1}^{-1}(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}\to p_{2}^{-1}f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}\to p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}.

By construction the projections
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
extend to morphisms of ringed topoi:

(p_{1},p_{1}^{\sharp})\colon((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\overline{\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})

and

(p_{2},p_{2}^{\sharp})\colon((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\overline{\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et}).

Lemma 6.12
.

The family of objects of the form

((E,J)\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))

(6.9)

is generating for
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
. Moreover if
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is smooth, then in addition one can take
\Spf(R)\to\Spf(S)
satisfying condition (2) of Definition
2.9
.

Proof.

Choose any object
T=((E,J)\to W\leftarrow V)
in
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
. First we reduce to the case
W
is affine. Take an affine Zariski covering of
W
which by pullback (and after possibly refining) gives an affine Zariski covering of
\Spf(E/J)
. Since
\Spf(E/J)
is quasi-compact, we can take a finite subfamily of the latter, which still covers
\Spf(E/J)
. By taking a disjoint union of the elements of this finite subfamily we obtain an étale covering of
\Spf(E/J)
, which is a singleton. Applying Lemma
6.3
to this singleton cover gives a covering of
(E,J)
, which factors through an affine
\Spf(S)
étale over
W
by construction. Finally by using coverings of type (c), we can assume
W
is affine. Coverings of type (b) give the desired generating family. In the case
f
is smooth one uses (a
\Spf(D/I)
-version of) Lemma
2.15
and coverings of type (b) to further refine.
∎

As a reality check, we now turn to calculating the sections of
\overline{\mathcal{O}}
.

Lemma 6.13
.

For
Z
of the form (
6.9
),
\overline{\mathcal{O}}(Z)=E/J\widehat{\otimes}_{S}R
, where the completion is
p
-adic.

Proof.

For each
n\geq 1
, consider first the uncompleted version modulo
p^{n}
:

\mathscr{F}_{n}:=(p_{1}^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\otimes_{p_{1}^{-1}(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y},\et}}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X},\et})/p^{n}.

Then
\mathscr{F}_{n}
is the sheafification of the presheaf
Z\mapsto(E/J\otimes_{S}R)/p^{n}

which is a sheaf by
[
ILO14
, Lemme 1.2]
. The result now follows by taking the limit.
∎

6.2
The object
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

In this section we construct the relative version of the
q
-crystalline complex
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
living in our topos (
6.5
) and record a few of its properties.

Lemma 6.14
.

There is a functor of topoi

\nu_{f}^{q}\colon(\mathfrak{X}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}

(6.10)

such that for any sheaf
\mathscr{F}
in
(\mathfrak{X}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
and any
Z
of the form (
6.9
)

(\nu_{f*}^{q}\mathscr{F})(Z)=\Gamma((E/J\widehat{\otimes}_{S}R)/E)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathscr{F})

Proof.

In addition to the topos
\widetilde{P}_{\et}:=(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
, the topoi

\widetilde{P}_{\Et}:=(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\Et}\text{ and }\widetilde{P}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}:=(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}}\mathfrak{X}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}

will also be useful. All three have explicit sites described by the formalism of §
6.1
,
P_{\et}
,
P_{\Et}
and
P_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
, respectively. Consider the diagram of sites

q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{X}/D)\xrightarrow{\nu}P_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}\xrightarrow{i}P_{\Et}\xleftarrow{j}P_{\et},

(6.11)

where the first functor is given by
(E,J)\mapsto((E,J)\to\Spf(E/J)\leftarrow\Spf(E/J))
and the remaining two functors are induced by the natural inclusions

\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\Et}\leftarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{\et}

for
\mathfrak{Z}\in\{\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{Y}\}
. The functor
\nu
is both continuous and cocontinuous, and
i
and
j
both induce morphisms of sites (moreover
i
induces an equivalence of topoi). Treating
\nu
as a cocontinuous functor, (
6.11
) induces the morphism of topoi (
6.10
) (as usual, one switches the adjoints for the equivalence induced by
i
). It remains to describe the pushforward of this morphism given by
j^{s}\circ i_{s}\circ{}_{s}\nu
. We compute

\displaystyle(j^{s}\circ i_{s}\circ{}_{s}\nu(\mathscr{F}))(Z)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}(i_{s}\circ{}_{s}\nu(\mathscr{F}))(j(Z))

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}({}_{s}\nu(\mathscr{F}))(j(Z))

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{=}\varprojlim_{\nu((E^{\prime},J^{\prime}))\to j(Z)}\mathscr{F}((E^{\prime},J^{\prime}))

where (i) is by definition of
j^{s}
, (ii) because
i
induces an equivalence of topoi (
j(Z)
is viewed as an object in
P_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
hereafter), and (iii) is by definition of
{}_{s}\nu
. To give a morphism
\nu((E^{\prime},J^{\prime}))\to j(Z)
is equivalent to giving a morphism of
q
-PD pairs
(E^{\prime},J^{\prime})\to(E,J)
in
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)
together with a compatible (over
\Spf(S)
) morphism
\Spf(E^{\prime}/J^{\prime})\to\Spf(R)
in
\mathfrak{X}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
. Thus the limit calculates precisely the global sections functor of the topos
((E/J\widehat{\otimes}_{S}R)/E)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
for
\mathscr{F}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma
6.14
allows us to make the following definition:

Definition 6.15
.

We define the relative
q
-crystalline complex

q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}:=R\nu_{f*}^{q}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. This is a
D
-algebra in
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
equipped with a
\phi_{D}
-semilinear endomorphism. In addition we set
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}):=Rp_{1*}q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
.

We now compare
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
with
relative
prismatic cohomology (cf.
[
BS22
, Theorem 16.17]
and
[
Kos21
, Theorem 7.13]
).

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
Z
be of the form (
6.9
) such that
\Spf(R)\to\Spf(S)
is a
p
-completely smooth
\Spf(D/I)
-morphism.
Letting
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}
be the
p
-completed base change along the map
E/J\to E/([p]_{q})
induced by
\phi_{E}
. Writing
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}/E}
for the prismatic cohomology of
(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}
relative to the bounded prism
(E,[p]_{q})
, there is a canonical isomorphism
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}/E}\cong R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
.

Proof.

The hard work is already done in
[
BS22
, §16]
. Indeed by Lemma
6.14

R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})=R\Gamma((R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)/E)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}).

(6.12)

The result now follows by Theorem 16.17 in loc.cit. and the comparison made in Lemma
6.6
.
∎

Finally we close this section by recording perfectness for
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
. In the
q
-crystalline setting one is able to obtain perfectness on the nose (without almost ambiguity). This is in contrast for the case of
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
(cf. Proposition
6.35
) and is an ubiquitous defect of the pro-étale topos associated to the generic fiber.

Proposition 6.17
.

Assume that we have a proper smooth
\Spf(D/I)
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes.
Then for each
(E,J)
in
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}

(1)

R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))
is perfect in
D(E)
.

(2)

R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\lvert_{(E,J)}
is strictly perfect in
D((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}/(E,J),\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\lvert_{(E,J)})
.

(3)

R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
is perfect in
D((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})
.

Proof.

We first show (i). In this proof we will abuse notation and denote
\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
for the structure sheaves on both
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
and
(\mathfrak{X}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. First note that
f
induces a cocontinuous functor of sites

\displaystyle f_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\colon q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{X}/D)

\displaystyle\to q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)

\displaystyle(E^{\prime},J^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mapsto(E^{\prime},J^{\prime})

and although
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
was originally defined via the intermediate oriented product, we can also write it as
Rf_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}*}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. Unwinding the definitions we get

R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))=R\Gamma((\Spf(E/J)\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X})/E)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}).

(6.13)

By a globalization
14
14

14

For the corresponding globalization in the log-smooth setting we refer to
[
Kos21
, Theorem 7.13]
.
of
[
BS22
, Theorem 16.17]
(obtained by sheafifying the result in loc.cit.) we get

R\Gamma((\Spf(E/J)\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X})/E)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})=R\Gamma((\Spf(E/[p]_{q})\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X})_{\et},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(\Spf(E/[p]_{q})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X})/E})

(6.14)

where
\Spf(E/[p]_{q})\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X}
is obtained by the
p
-completed base change of
\Spf(E/J)\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X}
along
E/J\to E/[p]_{q}
induced by
\phi_{E}
. Since
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
was assumed to be proper, the RHS of (
6.14
) is a perfect complex in
D(E)
by the Hodge-Tate comparison for prismatic cohomology (cf. Theorem 1.8(2) in loc.cit.). Thus (
6.13
) implies
R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))
is a perfect complex in
D(E)
for each
(E,J)
in
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
.

We now prove part (ii). This is a consequence of part (i) together with the
quasi-coherence
behaviour of prismatic/
q
-crystalline cohomology (cf. Theorem 1.8(5) in loc.cit.). Indeed these two points imply that the canonical morphism

\underline{R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))}\otimes_{E}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\lvert_{(E,J)}\to R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))\lvert_{(E,J)}

is a quasi-isomorphism. Finally part (iii) is an immediate consequence of part (ii).
∎

6.3
The Hodge-Tate comparison

In this short section we record the Hodge-Tate comparison morphism in the setting of the
q
-crystalline site (cf.
[
BS22
, Theorem 4.11]
and
[
Kos21
, Theorem 5.3]
) in the affine case. We then glue these morphisms to obtain a global statement. This will be a formal consequence of the Hodge-Tate comparison morphism constructed in
[
BS22
]
together with Theorem
6.16
. We assume
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is a smooth
\Spf(D/I)
-morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes.

Construction 6.18

(The Hodge-Tate comparison map: affine case)
.

Let
Z
be of the form (
6.9
). Denote by
\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/[p]_{q}
the complex whose
i
-th term is
\Omega_{R/S}^{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/[p]_{q}
(the completion is
p
-adic) with induced differentials coming from the de Rham complex. Now by the comparison with prismatic cohomology and
q
-crystalline cohomology (cf. Theorem
6.16
)

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}/E}\otimes_{E}^{\mathbb{L}}E/[p]_{q}\cong R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes_{E}^{\mathbb{L}}E/[p]_{q}.

(6.15)

Moreover by
[
BS22
, Theorem 4.11]
, we have an isomorphism of differential graded
E/[p]_{q}
-algebras

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}/(E/[p]_{q})}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\bullet}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}/E}\otimes_{E}^{\mathbb{L}}E/[p]_{q})\{\bullet\},

(6.16)

where the differentials on the RHS are induced by the Bockstein construction. Moreover by
[
FK18
, Chapter I, Proposition 5.1.5]

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/J)^{(1)}/(E/[p]_{q})}\cong\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/[p]_{q}.

(6.17)

Finally (
6.15
)-(
6.17
) together with functoriality of the Bockstein construction gives the following:

Corollary 6.19

(Hodge-Tate comparison: affine case)
.

For
Z
of the form (
6.9
) the map

\eta_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{q}(Z)\colon\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/[p]_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\bullet}(R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes_{E}^{\mathbb{L}}E/[p]_{q})\{\bullet\}

(6.18)

constructed above is an isomorphism of differential graded
E/[p]_{q}
-algebras.

We end this section by recording the globalization of Corollary
6.19
. In order to formulate the statement we will need appropriately twisted structure sheaves to accommodate the twist appearing in Theorem
6.16
. In particular we consider the variant
\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)}
defined by
(E,J)\mapsto E/[p]_{q}
. There is a morphism of sheaves of rings
\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)}
induced by Frobenius lifts. Similarly one defines
\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}
as in Definition
6.10
with
\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)}
in place of
\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
and we get an induced morphism of sheaves of rings
\overline{\mathcal{O}}\to\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}
. The morphism of sheaves of rings
p_{2}^{\sharp}
giving a morphism of ringed topoi

(p_{2},p_{2}^{\sharp(1)})\colon((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)})\to(\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}).

(6.19)

Let
p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\bullet}
denote the complex whose
i
th term is
p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{i}
and induced differentials (here
p_{2}
is considered as the twisted morphism of ringed topoi (
6.19
)). For the next result note that
p_{1}^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
is the sheaf
15
15

15

A priori
Z\mapsto E/[p]_{q}
is a presheaf, but one can easily verify the sheaf condition for each of the coverings of type (a)-(c). For coverings of type (a) this follows because
\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
is sheaf. Coverings of type (b)-(c) leave sections unchanged.

Z\mapsto E/[p]_{q}
.

Corollary 6.20

(Hodge-Tate comparison: global case)
.

The maps
\eta_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{q}(Z)
in Corollary
6.19
glue to give an isomorphism

\eta^{q}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\colon p_{2}^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\bullet}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{D}D/[p]_{q})\{\bullet\}

of differential graded
p_{1}^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
-algebras.

Proof.

Unravelling the definitions
p_{2}^{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is the sheafification of the presheaf

Z\mapsto\Omega_{R/S}^{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}E/[p]_{q}.

In addition, by taking an injective resolution of
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
, one shows that
H^{i}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{D}D/[p]_{q})\{i\}
is the sheafification of the presheaf

Z\mapsto H^{i}(R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes_{E}^{\mathbb{L}}E/[p]_{q})\{i\}.

The result now follows from Corollary
6.19
(for the glueing, one uses the functoriality of (
6.18
)).
∎

6.4
Base change for
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}

In this section we record the relevant base change for later use, namely when comparing relative
A_{\Inf}
-cohomology with
q
-crystalline cohomology. We continue to use the twisted sheaves developed in the previous section. For what follows we consider the commutative diagram of (twisted) ringed topoi

\textstyle{((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(p_{2},p_{2}^{\sharp(1)})}}
\scriptstyle{(p_{1},p_{1}^{\sharp(1)})}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{X}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f,f^{\sharp})}
\textstyle{((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(u_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{q},u_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{q\sharp(1)})}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{Y}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}).}

(6.20)

As usual we handle the affine case first.

Lemma 6.21
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are in addition affine.
Then for each
n\geq 1
the canonical base change map induced by (
6.20
)

u_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{q*}f_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})\to p_{1*}p_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})

(6.21)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Recall
u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
is induced by the diagram of sites

q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)\xrightarrow{g_{1}}\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}\xrightarrow{a}\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}\xleftarrow{g}\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}

and so
(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}=g_{1}^{s}a^{s}g_{s}
. Unravelling the definitions, the LHS of (
6.21
) is the sheafification of the presheaf

(E,J)\mapsto\varinjlim_{\Spf(E/J)\to g(W)}((\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})(W\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}(W)}E/[p]_{q}).

(6.22)

Since
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are affine, the expression inside the limit of (
6.22
) is in fact independent of
W
(for
W
affine) and so (
6.22
) simplifies to the sheafification of

(E,J)\mapsto(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})(\mathfrak{X})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}(\mathfrak{Y})}E/[p]_{q}.

(6.23)

We now deal with the RHS of (
6.21
). First note that
p_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})
is the sheaf

Z\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}(Z)/p^{n}=(E/[p]_{q}\otimes_{S}R)/p^{n}

where
Z
is of the form (
6.9
). Therefore
p_{1*}p_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n})
is the sheaf

(E,J)\mapsto(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}(\mathfrak{X})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}(\mathfrak{Y})}E/[p]_{q})/p^{n}.

(6.24)

Clearly (
6.23
) coincides with (
6.24
).
∎

We will also need vanishing of cohomology.

Lemma 6.22
.

Let
Z
be of the form (
6.9
). The cohomology groups
H^{i}(Z,\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}/p^{n})
vanish for
i>0
and
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

It suffices to observe that any covering of
Z
induces an étale covering of
\Spf(E/[p]_{q}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}R)
. The result now follows by vanishing of high Čech cohomology for affine schemes.
∎

Proposition 6.23
.

Suppose
f
is proper and smooth,
and
\mathscr{F}
a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}/p^{n}
-module of finite type for some
n\geq 1
. Then the canonical base change map induced by (
6.20
)

Lu_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{q*}Rf_{*}\mathscr{F}\to Rp_{1*}Lp_{2}^{*}\mathscr{F}

(6.25)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the proof of the counterpart in the pro-étale setting (cf. Proposition
5.24
) and so we omit the details. In the current situation however, we get a result on the nose because:

(1)

We know the sections of
\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}/p^{n}
(in the pro-étale setting the sections of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}/p^{n}
were only known up to almost ambiguity). This led us to a result on the nose of the affine case (cf. Lemma
6.21
).

(2)

By the proof of Lemma
6.22
we know the vanishing of higher Čech cohomology of
\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{(1)}/p^{n}
for coverings of
Z
of the form (
6.9
). The analogous result in the pro-étale setting (cf. the proof of Lemma
5.5
(i)) was only known up to almost ambiguity.

∎

6.5
q
-crystalline vs pro-étale fibered products

In this section we specialize to the case
(D,I)=(A_{\Inf},(\xi))
and assume that we have a
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b). In §
6.1
, we constructed the site

q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}

(6.26)

On the pro-étale side, in §
2.1
, we construced a site
D
. Let
D^{\prime}
be the subsite of
D
consisting of objects appearing in Proposition
2.8
. In this case,
D
and
D^{\prime}
have the same associated topoi:
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
. We want to compare the
q
-crystalline complex
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Definition
6.15
) and
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
. These two objects live in different topoi
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
and
Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}
, respectively. So we need to first construct a morphism between the topoi.

Lemma 6.24
.

The functor

\displaystyle u\colon D^{\prime}

\displaystyle\to q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}

\displaystyle(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))

\displaystyle\mapsto((A_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))\to\Spf(S)\leftarrow\Spf(R))

(6.27)

is cocontinuous.

Remark 6.25
.

We explain how the morphism
\Spf(S_{\infty})\to\Spf(S)
is defined so that the RHS of (
6.24
) makes sense. Let
S^{+}
be the integral closure of
S
in
S[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. Then the LHS of (
6.24
) gives a morphism
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spa(S[\frac{1}{p}],S^{+})
in
Y_{\proet}
. This gives a continuous morphism
S^{+}\to\varinjlim S_{i}^{+}
. The composition
S\to S^{+}\to\varinjlim S_{i}^{+}\to S_{\infty}
gives the desired morphism.

Proof.

We show the cocontinuity for coverings of type (a) first. Let
(E_{1},J_{1})\to(A_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))
be a covering in
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/(A_{\Inf})
. We verify the cocontinuity condition for this cover. By definition we have a
p
-completely étale covering map
\Spf(E_{1}/J_{1})\to\Spf(S_{\infty})
. So
E_{1}/J_{1}
is the
p
-adic derived completion of an étale
S_{\infty}
-algebra
\tfrac{S_{\infty}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]}{(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})}
. Then

E_{1}/(p,J_{1})=(S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p)[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]/(\overline{f}_{1},\ldots,\overline{f}_{n})\otimes_{S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p}\varinjlim_{i\in I}S_{i}^{+}/p

for some
i_{1}\in I
. Moreover by increasing
i_{1}
if necessary, we can assume
\tfrac{(S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p)[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]}{(\overline{f}_{1},\ldots,\overline{f}_{n})}
is faithfully flat over
S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p
(cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 07RR]
). By
[
Sta18
, Tag 04D1]
there exists some lift of
\tfrac{(S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p)[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]}{(\overline{f}_{1},\ldots,\overline{f}_{n})}
to an étale
S_{i_{1}}^{+}
-algebra
T
. Then
E_{1}/J_{1}
is the
p
-adic derived completion of
T\otimes_{S_{i_{1}}^{+}}\varinjlim_{i\in I}S_{i}^{+}
. We claim that

\Spa(\widehat{T}[\tfrac{1}{p}],T^{\prime})\to\Spa(S_{i_{1}},S_{i_{1}}^{+}),

where
\widehat{T}
is the
p
-adic completion of
T
, and
T^{\prime}
is the integral closure of
\widehat{T}
in
\widehat{T}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
, is an étale covering map. Indeed it is étale by
[
Hub96
, Corollary 1.7.3(iii)]
. It is also a covering because
T/p
is faithfully flat over
S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p
. It follows that the fiber product

\Spa(\widehat{T}[\tfrac{1}{p}],T^{\prime})\times_{\Spa(S_{i_{1}},S_{i_{1}}^{+})}\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})

(6.28)

exists in
Y_{\proet}
and is a covering of
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})
. We can rewrite (
6.28
) as

\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(\widehat{T}[\tfrac{1}{p}]\widehat{\otimes}_{S_{i_{1}}}S_{i},{S^{\prime}_{i}}^{+})

where
{S^{\prime}_{i}}^{+}
is the integral closure of
T^{\prime}\otimes_{S_{i_{1}}^{+}}S_{i}^{+}
in
\widehat{T}[\tfrac{1}{p}]\widehat{\otimes}_{S_{i_{1}}}S_{i}
. Therefore we obtain a composition of maps

T\otimes_{S_{i_{1}}^{+}}\varinjlim_{i\in I}S_{i}^{+}\to\varinjlim_{i\in I}(T^{\prime}\otimes_{S_{i_{1}}^{+}}S_{i}^{+})\to\varinjlim{S^{\prime}_{i}}^{+}.

This verifies the cocontinuty condition for coverings of type (a).

Coverings of type (b) are trivial to handle. We now deal with coverings of type (c): let
((A_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))\to\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathfrak{X}^{\prime})
be such a cover of the RHS of (
6.24
). By using the argument in the proof of Lemma
6.12
, we can refine this to a cover of the form
((A_{\Inf}(S^{\prime}_{\infty}),(\xi))\to\Spf(S^{\prime})\leftarrow\Spf(R^{\prime}))
where
\Spf(S^{\prime}_{\infty})\to\Spf(S_{\infty})
is an étale covering. This is a covering of type (a) followed by a covering of type (c). Thus by the first part of this proof, we have reduced to checking the cocontinuity condition for the case where we have a cover of the form
((A_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))\to\Spf(S^{\prime})\leftarrow\Spf(R^{\prime}))
of type (c) of the RHS of (
6.24
). This means that
\Spf(S_{\infty})\to\Spf(S)
factors through
\Spf(S^{\prime})\to\Spf(S)
. It suffices to show that
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to\Spa(S[1/p],S^{+})
in
Y_{\proet}
factors through
\Spa(S^{\prime}[\tfrac{1}{p}],S^{\prime+})\to\Spa(S[\tfrac{1}{p}],S^{+})
, where
S^{\prime+}
is the integral closure of
S^{\prime}
in
S^{\prime}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. Observe that a composition
\Spf(S_{\infty})\to\Spf(S^{\prime})\to\Spf(S)
is equivalent to a continuous
S
-morphism
S^{\prime}\to S_{\infty}
. Since
S/p\to S^{\prime}/p
is of finite presentation, there exists
i_{1}\in I
such that
S^{\prime}/p\to\varinjlim_{i\in I}S^{+}_{i}/p
factors through
S^{+}_{i_{1}}/p
. Moreover by increasing
i_{1}
if necessary, we can assume
S\to\varinjlim_{i\in I}S^{+}_{i}
factors through
S_{i_{1}}^{+}
. Then for each
n\geq 1
we have a commutative square

\textstyle{S^{\prime}/p^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{+}_{i_{1}}/p}
\textstyle{S/p^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{+}_{i_{1}}/p^{n},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the left vertical arrow is étale and so there is a unique map
S^{\prime}/p^{n}\to S_{i_{1}}^{+}/p^{n}
rendering the diagram commutative. Thus upon taking limits we obtain a continuous map
S^{\prime}\to S^{+}_{i_{1}}
compatible with the maps from
S
. The result now follows.
∎

Lemma
6.24
induces a morphism of topoi

\mu\colon Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}\to(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}.

(6.29)

6.6
Comparison with
q
-de Rham cohomology

In this short section we compare
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
with a
relative

q
-de Rham complex (cf.
[
BS22
, Theorem 16.22]
and
[
Kos21
, Theorem 7.17]
). We work locally and suppose
\Spf(R)\to\Spf(S)
is a smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism. In addition we assume
R
is
very small
over
S
, i.e.
R/p
is generated over
S/p
by units. This means that there is a canonical functorial surjection
P\to R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
with kernel
J
, where
P:=\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})[\{t_{s}\}_{s\in R^{\times}}]^{\wedge}
and the completions are
(p,\tilde{\xi})
-adic. In this context
