q=[\epsilon]\in A_{\Inf}
and in order to define
q
-de Rham cohomology in the case that we need, we verify the following result.

Lemma 6.26
.

\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
is a flat
A
-algebra.

Proof.

By
[
BMS18
, Remark 4.31]
, it suffices to show that
S_{\infty}^{\flat}
is
(\epsilon-1)
-torsion free. This follows from Lemma
4.1
and
[
CS19
, (2.1.2.2)]
.
∎

We may regard
P
as a
\delta
-
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
-algebra in a unique way by setting
\delta(x_{s})=0
for each
s\in R^{\times}
. Let
D_{J,q}(P)
be the
q
-PD envelope of
P\to R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}
. The main result is the following.

Theorem 6.27
.

Let
Z
be of the form the RHS of (
6.24
) such that in addition
R
is very small over
S
. Then there is a canonical functorial quasi-isomorphism
R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\cong q\Omega^{*,\square}_{D_{J,q}(P)/\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})}
.

Proof.

As with the comparison with prismatic cohomology, the hard work is already done in
[
BS22
, §16]
. Indeed by Lemma
6.14

R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})=R\Gamma((R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty})/\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}))_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}).

The result now follows by Theorem 16.21 in loc.cit. and the comparison made in Lemma
6.6
.
∎

6.7
Comparison with
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

We specialize to the case
(D,I)=(A_{\Inf},(\xi))
. Having established the following ingredients:

(1)

a way to pass from the pro-étale setting to the
q
-crystalline setting (cf. (
6.29
)),

(2)

the Hodge-Tate comparison for
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Corollary
6.19
), and

(3)

the Hodge-Tate comparison for
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Theorem
5.19
),

in this section we are now ready to compare
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
and
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf.
[
BS22
, Theorem 17.2]
and
[
Kos21
, Theorem 8.1]
).

Theorem 6.28
.

Assume that we have a smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b). Suppose
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat over
\mathcal{O}
. There is a canonical functorial morphism

\mu^{-1}q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\to A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

(6.30)

in
D(D,p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
compatible with the Frobenius such that the cohomology sheaves of the cone of (
6.30
)
are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})\subset A_{\Inf}
. If in addition
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then (
6.30
) is an isomorphism.

Remark 6.29
.

We explain how to view the LHS and RHS of (
6.30
) as objects in
D(D,p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
. For the RHS this is already explained in Definition
2.18
together with Lemma
2.19
. For the LHS, first note that
p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})=\mu^{-1}p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. Moreover, by temporarily regarding
\nu_{f}^{q}
as the following morphism of ringed topoi (we abuse notation and use
\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
to denote the structure sheaves on both
(\mathfrak{X}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
and
(\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
)

(\nu_{f}^{q},\nu_{f}^{q\sharp})\colon((\mathfrak{X}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})\to((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})

naturally equips
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
as an object in
D(q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/D)\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})
.

Proof.

We follow the main ideas of the proof of
[
BS22
, Theorem 17.2]
. Let
Z
be of the form the LHS of (
6.24
). One difference in the (current) relative case is that the derived
Z
-sections of the presheaf
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
identify with the derived
Z
-sections of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
only up to almost ambiguity (cf. (
5.20
)). For this reason (together with the fact that the derived
Z
-sections of the presheaf version are explicit), we will work directly with the presheaf.

We will use the notation developed in §
5.1
. In particular, addition to the site
D
, the site
D^{\text{psh}}
will be useful. In fact we will work with the subsites
D_{\text{vs}}\subset D
and
D^{\text{psh}}_{\text{vs}}\subset D^{\text{psh}}
consisting only of good objects
Z
such that
R
is very small over
S
. Note that
D_{\text{vs}}
and
D
induce the same topoi. We have
\mu^{-1}q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}=\phi^{-1}q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
where
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}
is the complex of presheaves given by

Z\mapsto R\Gamma(u(Z),q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}),

where
u
is the functor in Lemma
6.24
.
By (
5.2
), it suffices to construct a canonical functorial morphism

q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}\to A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}}

(6.31)

in
D(D^{\text{psh}}_{\text{vs}},p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
compatible with the Frobenius, whose cone satisfies the relevant conditions (we abuse notation and denote
p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})
for the presheaf
Z\mapsto\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
). We do this in several steps. We assume henceforth that
R
is very small over
S
.

Step 1: Constructing explicit representatives for
R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
and
R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
.

We rewrite each term
R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
and
R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
by explicit representatives of Koszul complexes. Strictly speaking this will be only up to almost ambiguity for the second term in general, and is one difference compared to the cases of
[
BS22
]
and
[
Kos21
]
. For the term
R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
, this is made possible by comparison with the
q
-de Rham complex (cf. Theorem
6.27
). Indeed
R\Gamma(u(Z),q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\cong q\Omega^{*,\square}_{D_{J,q}(P)/\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})}
and by definition
q\Omega^{*,\square}_{D_{J,q}(P)/\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})}
is the complex

\Kos_{c}(D_{J,q}(P);\{\nabla_{q,s}\}_{s\in R^{\times}})

(6.32)

defined as the term-wise
(p,[p]_{q})
-completed filtered colimit over all finite subsets
\Sigma\subset R^{\times}
of the corresponding cohomological Koszul complexes for
\{\nabla_{q,s}\}_{s\in\Sigma}
. The treatment of the term
R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
is slightly more delicate. We use the notation from §
4
. For each finite subset
\Sigma\subset R^{\times}
containing
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}
, there is a corresponding profinite group
\Delta_{\Sigma}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\Sigma}
with generators (over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
)
\{\sigma_{s}\}_{s\in\Sigma}
and a pro-(finite étale) affinoid perfectoid
\Delta_{\Sigma}
-cover
16
16

16

One takes
R^{\square}_{\Sigma}:=S\{\{t_{s}^{\pm 1}\}_{s\in\Sigma}\}
and defines
R^{\square}_{\Sigma,m}
, and
R_{\Sigma,\infty}
analogously as in (
4.1
) and (
4.3
), respectively. For instance
R^{\square}_{\Sigma,m}
contains a
p^{m}
-th root
t_{s}^{1/p^{m}}
.

\Spa(R_{\Sigma,\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}],R_{\Sigma,\infty})\to X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}\hskip 5.69054pt\text{ that refines the $\Delta=\Delta_{\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\}}$-cover }\hskip 5.69054ptX_{\infty}\to X\times_{Y}Y_{\infty}

compatible with the natural surjection
\Delta_{\Sigma}\twoheadrightarrow\Delta
. Consider now the complex

(\varinjlim_{\Sigma}\eta_{q-1}\Kos_{c}(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\Sigma,\infty});\{\sigma_{s}-1\}_{s\in\Sigma}))^{\wedge}

defined as the term-wise
(p,[p]_{q})
-completed filtered colimit over all finite subsets
\Sigma\subset R^{\times}
(containing
t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}
) of the corresponding cohomological Koszul complexes. By Theorem
4.11
, Remark
4.12
and (
5.3
), there is a morphism

(\varinjlim_{\Sigma}\eta_{q-1}\Kos_{c}(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\Sigma,\infty});\{\sigma_{s}-1\}_{s\in\Sigma}))^{\wedge}\to R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})

(6.33)

whose cone has its cohomology groups killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
(if
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then it is a quasi-isomorphism).

Step 2: Constructing a map
R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})\to R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
.
By (
6.32
)-(
6.33
), it is enough to construct a functorial comparison map

\Kos_{c}(D_{J,q}(P);\{\nabla_{q,s}\}_{s\in R^{\times}})\to(\varinjlim_{\Sigma}\eta_{q-1}\Kos_{c}(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\Sigma,\infty});\{\sigma_{s}-1\}_{s\in\Sigma}))^{\wedge}.

(6.34)

By
[
BMS18
, Lemma 7.9]
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\Kos_{c}(D_{J,q}(P);\{\nabla_{q,s}\}_{s\in R^{\times}})\cong\eta_{q-1}\Kos_{c}(D_{J,q}(P);\{\gamma_{s}-1\}_{s\in R^{\times}})

and so to construct (
6.34
),
it suffices to construct a canonical map

D_{J,q}(P)\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}((\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge})

(6.35)

of
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
-algebras that intertwines
\sigma_{s}
and
\gamma_{s}
(the completion appearing on the RHS is
p
-adic). We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}((\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}S_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge},}

where the top map is induced by mapping
t_{s}
to
[(\ldots,\tilde{t}_{s}^{1/p},\tilde{t}_{s})]
(where
\tilde{t}_{s}^{1/p^{m}}
denotes the image of
t_{s}^{1/p^{m}}\in R^{\square}_{\Sigma,m}
in
(\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge}
). The action of
\sigma_{s}
on
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}((\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge})
can be computed as in the last paragraph of §
4.2
(where it is done in the case
s=t_{i}
on
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
). This action clearly intertwines with the action of
\gamma_{s}
on
P
. Moreover
P\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}((\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge})
is a
\delta
-map and so extends uniquely to a
\delta
-map
D_{J,q}(P)\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}((\varinjlim_{\Sigma}R_{\Sigma,\infty})^{\wedge})
. The uniqueness also implies that it intertwines
\sigma_{s}
and
\gamma_{s}
(to see this one considers the compositions
P\xrightarrow{\gamma_{s}}P\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
and
P\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})\xrightarrow{\sigma_{s}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(R_{\infty})
). In summary we have constructed the desired map (
6.35
).

Step 3: Showing the cone of map
R\Gamma(Z,q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})\to R\Gamma(Z,A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\text{psh}})
from Step 2 satisfies the relevant conditions.
It suffices to check that the map (
6.34
) has cone whose cohomology groups are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
(and a quasi-isomorphism if
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
). We use (a variant of) the criterion given in
[
BS22
, Lemma 17.4]
. Let
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
be the source and target of (
6.34
). By the Hodge-Tate comparison for
q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Corollary
6.19
) there is an isomorphism

\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\bullet}(K_{1}/[p]_{q})\{\bullet\}

of differential graded
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}
-algebras. Let

\eta_{K_{1}}\colon R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}\to H^{0}(K_{1}/[p]_{q})

(6.36)

be the morphism in degree 0. In the proof of the de Rham specialization (cf. Theorem
5.20
) we constructed an isomorphism

\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}=(q^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})(Z)\to C^{\bullet}_{Z}.

(6.37)

Note that the twist appearing in
\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}
was implicit and is coming from (
5.7
).
Moreover by Remark
4.12
, there is a natural morphism

C^{\bullet}_{Z}\to H^{\bullet}(K_{2}/[p]_{q})\{\bullet\}

(6.38)

whose cone has its cohomology groups killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
(if
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
then it is an isomorphism). Composing (
6.37
)-(
6.38
) gives

\Omega_{R/S}^{\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}\to H^{\bullet}(K_{2}/[p]_{q})\{\bullet\}

(6.39)

whose cone has cohomology groups killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
in general, and if
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then it is an isomorphism.
Let

\eta_{K_{2}}\colon R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}\to H^{0}(K_{2}/[p]_{q})

(6.40)

be the morphism in degree 0 of (
6.39
). We remark that
\eta_{K_{2}}
is
S_{\infty}
-linear. To see this, note that (
6.38
) is clearly
S_{\infty}
-linear in each degree and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{D}(Z)\to C^{0}_{Z}
is
S_{\infty}
-linear as mentioned in the proof of Theorem
5.20
. One then checks that the composition induced by (
6.34
) and (
6.36
)

R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}\xrightarrow{\eta_{K_{1}}}H^{0}(K_{1}/[p]_{q})\to H^{0}(K_{2}/[p]_{q})

identifies with
\eta_{K_{2}}
. This can be done by reducing to the case
R=S\{t_{1}^{\pm 1}\}
where one uses that the morphisms are
S_{\infty}
-linear and keeps track of where the coordinate
t_{1}
is mapped to. Finally by a similar argument to the proof of
[
BS22
, Lemma 17.4]
(where in place of derived Nakayama, one uses the
almost
version, cf. Lemma
6.30
), one obtains that (
6.34
) has cone whose cohomology groups are killed by
W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
(and is a quasi-isomorphism if
\mathfrak{Y}
is locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
by loc.cit.).

In summary
Steps 1-3
show that the morphism (
6.34
) constructed is functorial in
Z
and satisfies the relevant conditions. Similarly by fixing an injective resolution
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,X}\to I^{\bullet}
in
X_{\proet}
, the edge map (
4.15
) comes from a functorial morphism of actual complexes. This implies that (
6.33
) globalizes. This produces the map (
6.31
) as promised.
∎

Almost in the next statement is w.r.t.
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]\subset A_{\Inf}
.

Lemma 6.30

(almost derived Nakayama)
.

Let
(\mathscr{C},A_{\Inf})
be a ringed site and suppose that
\mathscr{A}\in D(\mathscr{C},A_{\Inf})
is almost derived
[p]_{q}
-adically complete. If
\mathscr{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}
is almost zero, then so is
\mathscr{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
n\geq 1
, one obtains that
\mathscr{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}^{n}
is almost zero. Hence by taking the derived limit, we get that
R\varprojlim_{n}(\mathscr{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}^{n})
is almost zero. But by assumption the canonical morphism
\mathscr{A}\to R\varprojlim_{n}(\mathscr{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}^{n})
is an almost quasi-isomorphism and so the result follows.
∎

6.8
Comparison with
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})

In this section we again specialize to the case
(D,I)=(A_{\Inf},(\xi))
and unless otherwise stated, assume that we have a
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b). In addition we suppose
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat over
\mathcal{O}
. We now move to the comparison between (relative)
q
-crystalline cohomology (
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
, cf. Definition
6.15
) and
A_{\Inf}
-cohomology (
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
). Let
Y^{\prime}_{\proet}\subset Y_{\proet}
be the subsite consisting of affinoid perfectoid objects. By the proof of Lemma
6.24
, the functor of sites

\displaystyle u_{\proet}\colon Y^{\prime}_{\proet}

\displaystyle\to q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))

is cocontinuous and so induces a morphism of topoi

\mu_{\proet}\colon Y_{\proet}\to(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}.

We now have a priori, two different morphisms of topoi
u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}}\circ\mu_{\proet}
and
\nu_{Y}
from
Y_{\proet}
to
\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
. As a reality check, we verify that they coincide.

Lemma 6.31
.

The two morphisms of topoi
u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}}\circ\mu_{\proet}\colon Y_{\proet}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
and
\nu_{Y}\colon Y_{\proet}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}
are the same.

Proof.

Indeed for a sheaf
\mathscr{F}
on
Y_{\proet}
and
\mathfrak{V}
an affine object in
\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}

(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}*}\mu_{\proet,*}\mathscr{F})(\mathfrak{V})=\varprojlim_{\Spf(E/J)\to\mathfrak{V}}\varprojlim_{(A_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))\to(E,J)}\mathscr{F}(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+}))

(6.41)

where the left-most limit is taking place along
\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
and the middle limit along
q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})
. Combining these two limits implies the RHS of (
6.41
) can be rewritten as

\varprojlim_{\Spf(S_{\infty})\to\mathfrak{V}}\mathscr{F}(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+}))

with the left-most limit taking place along
\mathfrak{Y}_{\Et}^{\textnormal{Aff}}
. The morphism

\Spf(S_{\infty})\to\mathfrak{V}
is equivalent to giving a morphism on the generic fibers
\Spa(S_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}],S_{\infty})\to V
(in the category of adic spacs over
Y
) because
S_{\infty}
is integrally closed in
S_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. We claim that giving a morphism
\Spa(S_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}],S_{\infty})\to V
(in the category of adic spaces over
Y
) is equivalent to giving a morphism
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\to V
in
Y_{\proet}
. This is similar to the proof of the last part in Lemma
6.24
and we omit the details.
∎

Lemma 6.32
.

Assume that we have a proper smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b) with
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
flat over
\mathcal{O}
. Then the cohomology sheaves of the cone of the canonical morphism

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\to(\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))^{\wedge}

are killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]\subset A_{\Inf}
(the completion on the RHS is derived
[p]_{q}
-adic).

Proof.

Take a covering
\{U_{j}\to Y\}_{j}
by affinoid perfectoid objects of
Y
and write
U_{j}=\varprojlim_{i\in I_{j}}\Spa(S_{ij},S_{ij}^{+})
. Then

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\lvert_{U_{j}}=\mu_{\proet}^{-1}(R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\lvert_{(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}(S_{j,\infty}),(\xi))}).

By Proposition
6.17
(ii),
(R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\lvert_{(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}(S_{j,\infty}),(\xi))}
is represented by a strictly perfect object in
D((\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}/(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}(S_{j,\infty}),(\xi)),\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\lvert_{(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}(S_{j,\infty}),(\xi))})
. Now an easy calculation gives
\mu_{\proet}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}=W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})
. Moreover the cone of the canonical morphism

W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})\to\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,Y}

has its cohomology sheaves killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
(cf.
[
BMS18
, Lemma 5.6]
). Since
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,Y}
is derived
[p]_{q}
-complete by Corollary
2.28
, the result follows.
∎

Theorem 6.33
.

Assume that we have a proper smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b) with
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
flat over
\mathcal{O}
. There is a canonical functorial morphism

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\to R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})

(6.42)

in
D(Y_{\proet},W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
compatible with the Frobenius such that the cohomology sheaves of the cone of (
6.42
)
are killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]\subset A_{\Inf}
.

Remark 6.34
.

We explain how to view the LHS and RHS of (
6.42
) as objects in
D(Y_{\proet},W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
. For the RHS this is already explained in Definition
2.18
together with Lemma
2.19
. For the LHS, first note
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
is naturally an object of
D(q-\textnormal{CRYS}(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf}),\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})
. Since
\mu_{\proet}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}=W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})
, we get that the LHS can be viewed as an object in
D(Y_{\proet},W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
.

Proof.

The strategy is to work modulo
[p]_{q}
and to gradually massage the LHS towards the RHS of (
6.42
). The following schematic diagram summarises this “massage process”:

\textstyle{\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})/[p]_{q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\textnormal{I})}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\bullet}\lvert_{(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\textnormal{II})}
\textstyle{R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})/[p]_{q}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}^{\bullet}\lvert_{Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{(\textnormal{III})}

where steps (I) and (III) are combinations of the relevant Hodge-Tate comparisons, and step (II) is a combination of the base changes from the pro-étale and
q
-crystalline side. Finally after working modulo
[p]_{q}
, one can lift the results as both sides of (
6.42
) are (almost) derived
[p]_{q}
-adically complete. We now begin the proof.

We define (
6.42
) as the composition

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\to Rp_{*}\mu^{-1}q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\xrightarrow{Rp_{*}(\eqref{eq:compmorAOMeqOMe})}R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})

where the first map is induced by the canonical base change map for the commutative
17
17

17

Commutativity can be checked at the level of sites: by definition
\mu
and
\mu_{\proet}
are induced by cocontinuous functors and as mentioned in the proof of Lemma
6.9
, so are
p
and
p_{1}
.
diagram of ringed topoi

\textstyle{(Y_{\proet}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\overleftarrow{\times}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}\mathfrak{X}_{\et},p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{(Y_{\proet},W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\proet}}
\textstyle{((\mathfrak{Y}/D)_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}},\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}).}

(6.43)

Note that since the morphism (
6.30
) is constructed in
D(D,p^{-1}W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
, the morphism (
6.42
) is in
D(Y_{\proet},W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
.

By Corollary
5.13
,
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is almost derived
[p]_{q}
-adically complete and so the same is true for
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
. Similarly by Lemma
6.32
,
\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
is almost derived
[p]_{q}
-adically complete. Therefore by
almost derived Nakayama
(cf. Lemma
6.30
), it suffices to show that the induced morphism

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}\to R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}.

(6.44)

is an almost quasi-isomorphism. The LHS of (
6.44
) can be rewritten as

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}Rp_{1*}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}).

We have a spectral sequence

R^{i}p_{1*}H^{j}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})\implies R^{i+j}p_{1*}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})

whose terms on the second page satisfy

\displaystyle R^{i}p_{1*}H^{j}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\cong}R^{i}p_{1*}p_{2}^{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\cong}H^{i}(Lu^{q*}_{\mathfrak{Y}}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\})

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\cong}H^{i}((u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{(u^{q}_{\mathfrak{Y}})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)}),

where (a) follows from the (global) Hodge-Tate comparison (cf. Corollary
6.20
), (b) follows from base change (cf. Proposition
6.23
), noting that
\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\et}}
-module, and (c) is by definition of
u^{q*}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
. We compute further

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}_{\proet}R^{i}p_{1*}H^{j}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\cong}H^{i}(\nu_{Y}^{-1}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\nu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}}\mu^{-1}_{\proet}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)})

\displaystyle\overset{(2)}{\cong}H^{i}(\nu_{Y}^{-1}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\nu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y})\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}

\displaystyle\overset{(3)}{\cong}H^{i}(L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\})\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}

where (1) follows from Lemma
6.31
, (2) follows from the fact that
\mu^{-1}_{\proet}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}^{(1)}
is the sheaf
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})\mapsto\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/[p]_{q}
, and (3) follows by definition upon viewing
\nu_{Y}
as a morphism of ringed topoi
(Y_{\proet},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{Y})\to(\mathfrak{Y}_{\et},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}})
.

By Corollary
5.27
, the base change morphism

L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\to Rp_{*}Lq^{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}

is an almost isomorphism (via the canonical map
A_{\Inf}\to A_{\Inf}/\xi
,
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
is sent to
\mathfrak{m}
). Therefore the expression on the RHS of (3) is almost isomorphic to

R^{i}p_{*}q^{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}.

Moreover by the Hodge-Tate comparison (cf. Theorem
5.19
), there is an almost isomorphism

R^{i}p_{*}q^{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}\to R^{i}p_{*}H^{j}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}.

There is a spectral sequence (with second page terms)

R^{i}p_{*}H^{j}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}\implies Rp_{*}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})

By construction the morphism (
6.30
) was shown to be compatible with the Hodge-Tate comparisons on the
q
-crystalline side and the pro-étale side, and so we conclude that there is an almost quasi-isomorphism

\mu_{\proet}^{-1}Rp_{1*}(q\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})\to Rp_{*}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}).

(6.45)

compatible with (
6.44
). Finally by (
5.4
) the map

R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q}\to Rp_{*}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}/\xi,\varphi}A_{\Inf}/[p]_{q})

(6.46)

is an almost quasi-isomorphism (with the twist by
\varphi
being implicit there). Combining (
6.45
)-(
6.46
), gives that (
6.44
) is an almost quasi-isomorphism.
∎

6.9
Perfectness of
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})

In this short section we record (almost) perfectness of
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
together with base change (cf. Proposition
6.35
for the precise result). We will rely on the comparison with
q
-crystalline cohomology (cf. Theorem
6.33
), however similar results can be obtained (albeit via a longer argument) using only the de Rham specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Theorem
5.20
). Almost in the next proposition is with respect to
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]\subset A_{\Inf}
.

Proposition 6.35
.

Assume that we have a proper smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes with
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
flat over
\mathcal{O}
. Let
U\in Y_{\proet}
be an affinoid perfectoid object determining the perfectoid space
\Spa(R,R^{+})
. Then

(1)

R\Gamma(U,R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))
is almost quasi-isomorphic to a perfect object in
D(W(R^{\flat+}))
.

(2)

R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\lvert_{U}
is almost quasi-isomorphic to a strictly perfect object in
D(Y_{\proet}/U,W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}\lvert_{U}))
.

(3)

R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
is almost quasi-isomorphic to a perfect object in
D(Y_{\proet},W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
.

(4)

For a morphism of affinoid perfectoids
U^{\prime}\to U
(in
Y_{\proet}
) with
U^{\prime}
determining the perfectoid space
\Spa(R^{\prime},R^{\prime+})
, the natural morphism

R\Gamma(U,R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{W(R^{\flat+})}W(R^{\prime\flat+})\to R\Gamma(U^{\prime},R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))

has the cohomology groups of its cone killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
.

Proof.

Parts (i)-(iii) are an immediate consequence of

(1)

the analogous perfectness results of
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
(cf. Proposition
6.17
(i)-(iii)) and

(2)

the comparison of
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
with
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
(cf. Theorem
6.33
).

Finally part (iv) follows from part (ii).
∎

6.10
Comparison with relative
p
-adic étale cohomology

Assume that we have a proper smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes locally of finite type and flat over
\mathcal{O}
. In this section we compare
R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
with
p
-adic étale cohomology (i.e.
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
). This will involve considering the derived
p
-adic completions as
\mu\notin[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]
.

Theorem 6.36
.

We have an identification

(\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))^{\wedge}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]\cong Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{A}_{\Inf,Y}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}],

where the completion on the LHS is derived
p
-adic.

Proof.

Denote by
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})^{\wedge}
the derived
p
-adic completion of
R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})
. Then Theorem
6.33
and
[
CK19
, Lemma 3.17]
induces a quasi-isomorphism

(\mu_{\proet}^{-1}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))^{\wedge}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]\cong R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})^{\wedge}\otimes_{A_{\Inf}}^{\mathbb{L}}A_{\Inf}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]

as
\mu\in W(\mathfrak{m}^{\flat})
. The result now follows from Theorem
3.15
.
∎

7
Applications

7.1
Relative Hodge-Tate spectral sequence

In this section we record a relative Hodge-Tate spectral sequence in Theorem
7.2
and is independent of the
q
-crystalline section. This only uses the results developed in §
5
. In the context of a discretely valued complete nonarchimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with perfect residue field, such a sequence already exists by
[
CS17
, Corollary 2.2.4]
(cf.
[
AG22
, Remarque 1.4.7]
). In the context of schemes, results have been obtained in
[
AG22
]
. It is an interesting question to understand the compatibility of these spectral sequences. Our approach to establishing the relative Hodge-Tate spectral sequence mirrors that of loc.cit. (we both pass through a fiber product of topoi) of which the following table summarises the similarities of the two approaches.

Our approach

Approach of Abbes-Gros

Use of pro-étale site

Use of Faltings’ site

Hodge-Tate specialization of
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Theorems
5.2
and
5.19
)

Hodge-Tate comparison of the
structure sheaf
of the relative Faltings’ topos (cf.
[
AG22
, Théorème 6.6.4]
)

Base change for fiber product of topoi (cf. Proposition
5.24
)

Base change for the relative Faltings’ site (cf.
[
AG22
, Théorème 6.5.31]
)

Let us mention one difference between the two approaches is that we get rid of the “junk torsion” (via the décalage functor) while Abbes-Gros do not.

We begin by comparing the sheaf of Kähler differentials on the special and generic fibers.

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is a proper smooth morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b) over
\mathcal{O}
. Suppose
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat over
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
and
X
and
Y
are locally of finite type over
\Spa(C,\mathcal{O})
. Consider the commutative diagram of ringed topoi

\textstyle{(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\eta}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\Zar}}
\textstyle{(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}),}

and
\mathscr{F}
a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-module. Then the canonical base change morphism

L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}\to Rf_{\eta*}L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

First note that by
[
Sta18
, Tag 0A1H]
,
Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}/p^{n}
is a perfect object of
D(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}/p^{n})
and its formation commutes with base change. Therefore by taking the derived limit we obtain
Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}
itself is a perfect object of
D(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}})
. Therefore
L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}
is a perfect object of
D(\mathcal{O}_{Y})
.

The claim is local for the Zariski topology on
\mathfrak{Y}
, so we can assume it is affine, say
\mathfrak{Y}=\Spf(S)
.
Let
U\subseteq Y
be any open affinoid of
Y
such that
L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}\lvert_{U}
is strictly perfect. Then for any rational subset
U^{\prime}\subseteq U
, by Tate acyclicity

R\Gamma(U,L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U)}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U^{\prime})\cong R\Gamma(U^{\prime},L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}).

(7.1)

Moreover by Kiehl’s finiteness theorem
[
Kie67
, Theorem 3.3]

(R^{i}f_{\eta*}\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F})(U)\otimes_{O_{Y}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U^{\prime})=(R^{i}f_{\eta*}\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F})(U^{\prime}).

(7.2)

Thus by Tate acyclicity and flatness of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U^{\prime})
over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U)
(cf.
[
Hub94
, pg. 530]
), (
7.2
) implies

R\Gamma(U,Rf_{\eta*}L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U)}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(U^{\prime})\cong R\Gamma(U^{\prime},Rf_{\eta*}L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F}).

(7.3)

Therefore (
7.1
) and (
7.3
) implies that it suffices to check that the induced morphism

R\Gamma(U,L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F})\to R\Gamma(U,Rf_{\eta*}L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F})

is a quasi-isomorphism where in addition
U
comes from some affine open
\mathfrak{U}\subseteq\mathfrak{Y}
.

We can assume without loss of generality that
\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{Y}
and
Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F}
is represented by a strictly perfect complex
\mathscr{E}^{\bullet}
. In that case
R\Gamma(U,L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{Y}}^{*}Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F})
is represented by the complex
\mathscr{E}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{Y})\otimes_{S}S[\tfrac{1}{p}]
, which in turn is just
R\Gamma(\mathfrak{Y},Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F})\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}S[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. Choose some finite open affine cover of
\mathfrak{X}
. Then
R\Gamma(\mathfrak{Y},Rf_{\Zar*}\mathscr{F})
is computed via the associated Čech complex
C^{\bullet}
and
R\Gamma(U,Rf_{\eta*}L\text{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}\mathscr{F})
is computed by
C^{\bullet}\otimes_{S}S[\tfrac{1}{p}]
. Since
S[\tfrac{1}{p}]
is flat over
S
, the result now follows.
∎

Theorem 7.2
.

Suppose
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is a proper smooth morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b) over
\mathcal{O}
. Assume that
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat over
\mathcal{O}
. There is an
E_{2}
-spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}(L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\})\implies H^{i+j}(R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O})

(7.4)

in
D(Y_{\proet},\mathcal{O}^{a})
. Moreover, if both
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
then upon inverting
p
, (
7.4
) takes the shape

E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}(Rf_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-j))\implies R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}

(7.5)

in
D(Y_{\proet},C)
(here the
f_{\eta*}
is pushforward between analytic topologies). In addition, if for any
i,j\geq 0
,
R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}
is locally free of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, then (
7.5
) takes the shape

E_{2}^{i,j}=R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-j)\implies R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}

(7.6)

and it degenerates.

Remark 7.3
.

Let
K
be a discretely valued complete nonarchimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with perfect residue field and ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Then if
f_{\eta}
comes from base change of a proper smooth morphism of smooth adic spaces over
\Spa(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
, then by
[
Sch13
, Theorem 8.8(ii)]
and base change, for any
i,j\geq 0
,
R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}
is locally free of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.2
and the projection formula (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 0944]
) we get a morphism in
D(Y_{\proet},\mathcal{O})

R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O}\to Rp_{*}\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}

whose image in
D(Y_{\proet},\mathcal{O}^{a})
is an almost isomorphism. Therefore we obtain an
E_{2}
-spectral sequence (the Cartan-Leray spectral sequence)

R^{i}p_{*}H^{j}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})\implies H^{i+j}(R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O})

in
D(Y_{\proet},\mathcal{O}^{a})
. By Theorem
5.19
, we get a morphism
R^{i}p_{*}q^{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\to R^{i}p_{*}H^{j}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}})
in
D(Y_{\proet},\mathcal{O})
, whose image in
D(Y_{\proet},\mathcal{O}^{a})
is an almost isomorphism. By Corollary
5.27
, the morphism

H^{i}(L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\})\to R^{i}p_{*}q^{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}

is an almost isomorphism. This proves (
7.4
).

Suppose now both
X
and
Y
are locally of finite type over
\Spa(C,\mathcal{O})
. We now invert
p
and perform some yoga to obtain (
7.5
). Indeed

\displaystyle H^{i}(L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}C

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\cong}H^{i}(L\nu_{Y}^{*}Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\{-j\}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathbb{L}}C)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\cong}H^{i}(Rf_{*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{\et}}}^{\mathbb{L}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-j))

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\cong}H^{i}(Rf_{\Zar,*}\Omega^{j}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-j))

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\cong}H^{i}(Rf_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-j))

where (i) follows from localization being flat, (ii) is by definition of
L\nu_{Y}^{*}
(we have dropped the
\nu_{Y}^{-1}
) and the fact that the cokernel of
\mathcal{O}(1)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}\{1\}
is killed by
p^{1/(p-1)}
, (iii) from the fact that coherent cohomology can be computed in the étale or Zariski site, and (iv) is a consequence of Lemma
7.1
. The identification

H^{i+j}(R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta\circ\varphi^{-1}}\mathcal{O})[\tfrac{1}{p}]\cong R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}

follows from Theorem
3.15
(note that
\mu
is invertible in
A_{\Inf}/\tilde{\xi}
). This proves (
7.5
) of which (
7.6
) is a direct consequence.

We now prove degeneration of (
7.6
). By Lemma
3.12
the complex
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is adic lisse and in particular it is locally a perfect complex of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-modules. Thus

R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}=R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\tfrac{1}{p}]\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[\tfrac{1}{p}]}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}

is a locally free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-module. Recall that locally free sheaves of finite rank on
Y_{\proet}
correspond to locally free sheaves of finite rank on the analytic topology on
Y
by
[
Sch13
, Lemma 7.3]
). So the rank (locally) of such objects is determined by the rank at geometric points of rank 1. Fix an integer
m\geq 0
. By
[
Hub96
, Proposition 2.6.1]
applied
18
18

18

It is enough to look at
R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\et*}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
for each
n
as
Rf_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is adic lisse.
to
R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\et*}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
for each
n
, commutation with arbitrary base change for the terms
R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}
(by locally freeness assumption) and the degeneration of the Hodge-Tate spectral sequence (cf.
[
BMS18
, Theorem 13.3(ii)]
), we get a covering
\{U_{k}\to Y\}_{k}
in
Y_{\proet}
such that restricted to
U_{k}
each term in the quadrant
i+j\leq m
of the second page of (
7.6
) is free (as well as the abutment
R^{i+j}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
) and

\sum_{i+j=l}\textrm{rank}(R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{j}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-j)\lvert_{U_{k}})=\textrm{rank}(R^{l}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\lvert_{U_{k}})

(7.7)

for all
k
and any
0\leq l\leq m
. Since affinoid perfectoids form a basis for
Y_{\proet}
, we can assume further that each
U_{k}
is affinoid perfectoid and determines a perfectoid space
\Spa(S_{k},S_{k}^{+})
. Then by vanishing of higher cohomology (cf.
[
Sch13
, Lemma 4.10(v)]
), taking the
U_{k}
-sections of (
7.6
) gives a spectral sequence consisting of finite free
S_{k}
-modules. It suffices to show that the resulting spectral sequence degenerates. Consider a differential
d_{2}^{i,j}\colon E_{2}^{i,j}(U_{k})=S_{k}^{a}\to E_{2}^{i+2,j-1}(U_{k})=S_{k}^{b}
for any
i+j\leq m-1
. Then for each minimal prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
of
S_{k}
, the localization
S_{k,\mathfrak{p}}
is a field as
S_{k}
is reduced (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 00EU]
). Thus by (
7.7
),
d_{2}^{i,j}
localized at
\mathfrak{p}
is the zero morphism. Thus the induced morphism

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\text{ minimal}}(S_{k,\mathfrak{p}})^{a}\to\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\text{ minimal}}(S_{k,\mathfrak{p}})^{b},

(7.8)

where the products run over all minimal prime ideals of
S_{k}
, is the zero morphism. Moreover
S_{k}\to\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\text{ minimal}}S_{k,\mathfrak{p}}
is an embedding (cf.
[
Sta18
, Tag 00EW]
). Therefore
d_{2}^{i,j}
is the zero morphism. This proves degeneration.
∎

7.2
Torsion in étale pushforward

The result in this section makes use of the
q
-crystalline theory from §
6
. Although one could avoid this by using the de Rham specialization for
A\Omega_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}}
(cf. Theorem
5.20
), one would still need to prove a perfectness result such as Proposition
6.35
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Suppose
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is a proper smooth morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes locally of finite type and flat over
\mathcal{O}
. Fix some
j\geq 0
. Suppose that
R^{i}f_{\dR*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
-module for all
i\geq j
. Then for all
i\geq j

(1)

R^{i}f_{\eta,\proet*}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is
p
-torsion free,

(2)

if
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})
is an affinoid perfectoid of
Y_{\proet}
determining the perfectoid space
\Spa(S_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}],S_{\infty})
, then
H^{i}(R\Gamma((\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi)),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})))
is a finite locally free
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
-module and

(3)

if
\mathfrak{Y}
is in addition smooth and
(E,J)
is any object in
(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
, then

H^{i}(R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})))
is a finite locally free
E
-module.

Remark 7.5
.

A slight variant of part (2) is also proved in
[
MT20
, Lemma 1.38]
by another method. The key point in the argument of loc.cit. is to take a judicious base change to a rank 1 perfectoid valuation ring, at which point the result of
[
BMS18
, Theorem 1.8]
can be applied. We take a different approach and instead rely on the comparison with relative
p
-adic étale cohomology (cf. Theorem
6.36
). T. Tsuji also pointed out that (1) immediately follows from the analagous result in
[
BMS18
, Theorem 14.5(ii)]
(cf. Remark 14.4 in loc.cit.) together with
[
Hub96
, Proposition 2.6.1]
and Lemma
3.12
.

Proof.

We first show part (2) and we use the notation from §
6
. Let
(E,J)
be an object in
(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}
. Then by the de Rham comparison with
q
-crystalline/prismatic cohomology (cf.
[
BS22
, Corollary 15.4]
) and (
6.13
)

R\Gamma_{\dR}((\Spf(E/[p]_{q})\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X})/\Spf(E/[p]_{q}))\cong R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O}.

(7.9)

Note that by Proposition
6.17
(i), the RHS of (
7.9
) is already (
p
-adically derived) complete. Since
R^{i}f_{\dR*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
-module for all
i\geq j
and
Rf_{\dR*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is a perfect complex and commutes with arbitrary base change,
R^{i}f_{\dR*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
commutes with arbitrary base change and so
R^{i}f^{\prime}_{\dR*}\mathcal{O}_{\Spf(E/[p]_{q})\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X}}
is a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\Spf(E/[p]_{q})}
-module (here
f^{\prime}\colon\Spf(E/[p]_{q})\times_{\mathfrak{Y}}\mathfrak{X}\to\Spf(E/[p]_{q})
is obtained from base change) for all
i\geq j
. By affine vanishing of higher cohomology, it follows that
H^{i}
of the LHS of (
7.9
) is a finite locally free
E/[p]_{q}
-module for all
i\geq j
. Therefore

H^{i}(R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O})

(7.10)

is a finite locally free
E/[p]_{q}
-module for all
i\geq j
.

Let
\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid of
Y_{\proet}
determining the perfectoid space
\Spa(S_{\infty}[\tfrac{1}{p}],S_{\infty})
and suppose
(E,J)=(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi))
. We now proceed by descending induction on
i\geq j
to prove that

H^{i}:=H^{i}(R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})))

is a finite locally free
E
-module. Since
R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))
is a perfect complex in
D(E)
(cf. Proposition
6.17
(i)), it is quasi-isomorphic to a complex
E^{\bullet}
of finite projective
E
-modules with
E^{k}=0
for
k\geq m+1
for some
m\geq i
. Then
H^{m}
is a finite
E
-module and
H^{m}\otimes_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O}
coincides with (
7.10
) for
i=m
. Assume without loss of generality
H^{m}\otimes_{A_{\Inf},\theta}\mathcal{O}
is a finite free
E/[p]_{q}
-module and choose a basis
\overline{B}
(this does not affect the form of
(E,J)
, as it is enough to take a Zariski cover of
\mathfrak{Y}
). By Nakayama’s lemma, the basis
\overline{B}
lifts to a generating set
B
of
H^{m}
. Moreover by Proposition
6.17
(ii),
[
Sch13
, Theorem 6.5(ii)]
(in particular the sentence immediately following
[
BMS18
, Lemma 5.6]
) and the equality
\mu_{\proet}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}=W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y})
we get

H^{m}[\tfrac{1}{\mu}]=H^{m}(R\Gamma(\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+}),(\mu^{-1}_{\proet}R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y}))^{\wedge}))[\tfrac{1}{\mu}].

(7.11)

By Theorem

to

Part (1) then follows from Theorem

of

and a choice of

). Since
\mathfrak{Y}
is assumed to be smooth, we can take

to be a small
p
-completely smooth
\mathcal{O}
-algebra (recall this means that there is an étale map
\Spf(S)\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\})
). Above
\Spa(C\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\},\mathcal{O}\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\})
, we have the affinoid perfectoid covering

\varprojlim_{m\geq 0}\Spa(C\{t_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{m}},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{m}}\},\mathcal{O}\{t_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{m}},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{m}}\}).

The induced map
\mathcal{O}\{t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\}\to\mathcal{O}\{t_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\}
is quasisyntomic. The map

, induced by (

[

, Lemma 4.16(2)]
. Now suppose

factors through
\Spf(S)
. Then again by loc.cit. the induced map
E/\tilde{\xi}\to S_{\infty}\widehat{\otimes}_{S^{(1)}}E/\tilde{\xi}
is quasisyntomic (the completion is
p
-adic and

). By
[

, Proposition 7.11]
, there exists an object

(E^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}/\tilde{\xi}\leftarrow S_{\infty}\widehat{\otimes}_{S^{(1)}}E/\tilde{\xi})\in((S_{\infty}\widehat{\otimes}_{S^{(1)}}E/\tilde{\xi})/E)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},

and moreover the map
S_{\infty}\widehat{\otimes}_{S^{(1)}}E/\tilde{\xi})\to E^{\prime}/\varphi(\xi)
is
p
-completely faithfully flat. Therefore the composition
E/\tilde{\xi}\to E^{\prime}/\tilde{\xi}
is also
p
-completely faithfully flat.
By universality (cf. Lemma 4.7 in loc.cit.), the induced map

lifts uniquely to a map

of prisms. Therefore by part (2)

H^{i}(R\Gamma((\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi)),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})))\otimes_{\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})}E^{\prime}

(7.15)

is a finite locally free
E^{\prime}
-module. The expression (
7.15
) coincides with

H^{i}(R\Gamma((E,J),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})))\widehat{\otimes}_{E}E^{\prime},

where the completion is
(p,\tilde{\xi})
-adic (to see this, identify

-crystalline cohomology with prismatic cohomology (

)-(

) and apply

, Theorem 1.8(5)]
).
By a similar argument as the proof of

, Lemma A.12]
(in particular using Lemma A.8 of loc.cit.), one concludes that

E
-module. In general (in the case when the morphism
\Spf(E/J)\to\mathfrak{Y}
does not necessarily factor through
\Spf(S)
), there is a covering

, Lemma A.12]
, as before.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

In the setting of Theorem
7.4
, there is also a Frobenius structure on

H^{i}(R\Gamma((\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty}),(\xi)),R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})))

(7.16)

and by the isogeny theorem (cf.
[
BS22
, Theorem 1.8(6)]
), the linearization morphism (induced by Frobenius) is an isomorphism after inverting
\tilde{\xi}
.
Together with part (2) of Theorem
7.4
, the expression (
7.16
) gives rise to a
relative Breuil-Kisin-Fargues module
. This is made precise in
[
MT20
]
, where various categories (e.g.
\mu
-small generalised representations over
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
for a particular
S_{\infty}
and relative Breuil-Kisin-Fargues modules with Frobenius structure) are shown to be equivalent. In the language of loc.cit. (
7.16
) can be upgraded to a generalised representation over
\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})
(with the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)^{d}
-action
20
20

20

In this context, one takes a small Zariski neighbourhood
\Spf(S)
of
Y
which can be described using
d
-tori-coordinates. The
S
-algebra
S_{\infty}
is then obtained from
S
by adjoining all
p
-th roots of the tori-coordinates and this gives the usual
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)^{d}
-action on
\mathbb{A}_{\inf}(S_{\infty})
, which in turn induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)^{d}
-action on (
7.16
).
satisfying a certain triviality condition modulo
\mu
) with Frobenius structure.

7.3
The relative absolute crystalline comparison

Let
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
be a smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b). Assume that
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat over
\mathcal{O}
. In this section we prove a relative version of the absolute crystalline comparison isomorphism (cf.
[
BMS18
, Theorem 12.1]
).

As usual let
X:=\mathfrak{X}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O})}\Spec(\mathcal{O}/p)
and
Y:=\mathfrak{Y}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O})}\Spec(\mathcal{O}/p)
be the fibers modulo
p
of
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
, respectively. In addition let
\textnormal{CRYS}(X/A_{\textnormal{crys}})
and
\textnormal{CRYS}(Y/A_{\textnormal{crys}})
be the absolute (big) crystalline sites of
X
and
Y
, respectively. We denote the corresponding topoi by
(X/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}
(resp.
(Y/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}
). Let
\mathcal{O}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be the structure sheaf on
(X/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}
. The morphism
f
induces a functor of topoi

f^{\textnormal{CRYS}}\colon(X/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}\to(Y/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}.

Let
Y^{\prime}_{\proet}\subset Y_{\proet}
be the subsite consisting of affinoid perfectoid objects. By killing
\mu=q-1
in the target of
u_{\proet}
in §
6.8
, we obtain a cocontinuous functor of sites

\displaystyle u_{\proet}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}\colon Y^{\prime}_{\proet}

\displaystyle\to\textnormal{CRYS}(Y/A_{\textnormal{crys}})

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{i\in I}\Spa(S_{i},S_{i}^{+})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathbb{A}_{\Inf}(S_{\infty})/\mu,(\xi/\mu))

which induces a morphism of topoi

\mu_{\proet}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}\colon Y_{\proet}\to(Y/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}.

We can now state the absolute crystalline comparison in the current context:

Theorem 7.7
.

Assume that we have a smooth
\Spf(\mathcal{O})
-morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
of
p
-adic formal schemes of type (S)(b). Suppose in addition that
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}
are flat over
\mathcal{O}
. There is a canonical functorial morphism (the completion on the target is derived
p
-adic)

(\mu^{\textnormal{CRYS}}_{\proet})^{-1}Rf_{*}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}\mathcal{O}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to R\Gamma_{A_{\Inf}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\widehat{\otimes}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\textnormal{crys}}

(7.17)

in
D(D,W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+,\flat}_{Y}))
compatible with the Frobenius such that the cohomology sheaves of the cone of (
7.17
)
are killed by
[\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}]\subset A_{\Inf}
.

Proof.

Let
r\colon(\mathfrak{Y}/A_{\Inf})_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}\to(Y/A_{\textnormal{crys}})_{\textnormal{CRYS}}
be the natural morphism of topoi such that
\mu_{\proet}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}=r\circ\mu_{\proet}
. Then by
[
BS22
, Theorem 16.14]

r^{-1}Rf_{*}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}\mathcal{O}^{\textnormal{CRYS}}_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cong R\Gamma_{q-\textnormal{CRYS}}(\mathfrak{X}/\mathfrak{Y})\widehat{\otimes}_{A_{\Inf}}A_{\textnormal{crys}}
. The result now follows from Theorem
6.33
.
∎
